`benchmarks/unity/combat-formula/src/Assets/Editor/BenchmarkBuild.cs`:

```cs
using System;
using System.IO;
using UnityEditor;
using UnityEditor.Build;
using UnityEditor.Build.Reporting;
using UnityEngine;

public static class BenchmarkBuild
{
    [MenuItem("ReShield/Build Mono L0")]
    public static void Mono() { Build(ScriptingImplementation.Mono2x, "mono"); }

    [MenuItem("ReShield/Build IL2CPP L1")]
    public static void IL2CPP() { Build(ScriptingImplementation.IL2CPP, "il2cpp"); }

    private static void Build(ScriptingImplementation backend, string variant)
    {
        if (Application.unityVersion != "6000.3.8f1")
            throw new Exception("Benchmark pins Unity 6000.3.8f1; do not silently upgrade.");
        const string scenePath = "Assets/Scenes/Main.unity";
        // Build the checked-in scene. Never create, replace or save source scenes here.
        if (!File.Exists(Path.Combine(Application.dataPath, "Scenes/Main.unity")))
            throw new FileNotFoundException("Restore the benchmark scene before building.", scenePath);
        for (int i = 0; i < UnityEngine.SceneManagement.SceneManager.sceneCount; i++)
            if (UnityEngine.SceneManagement.SceneManager.GetSceneAt(i).isDirty)
                throw new Exception("Save or discard scene edits yourself before building; the benchmark will not save them.");
        string sample = Directory.GetParent(Application.dataPath).Parent.FullName;
        string output = Path.Combine(sample, "builds", variant);
        Directory.CreateDirectory(output);
        PlayerSettings.companyName = "ReShield";
        PlayerSettings.productName = "CombatBenchmark";
        PlayerSettings.runInBackground = true;
        PlayerSettings.SetScriptingBackend(NamedBuildTarget.Standalone, backend);
        EditorUserBuildSettings.development = false;
        EditorUserBuildSettings.allowDebugging = false;
        EditorUserBuildSettings.connectProfiler = false;
        EditorBuildSettings.scenes = new[] { new EditorBuildSettingsScene(scenePath, true) };
        var report = BuildPipeline.BuildPlayer(new BuildPlayerOptions {
            scenes = new[] { scenePath },
            locationPathName = Path.Combine(output, "CombatBenchmark.exe"),
            target = BuildTarget.StandaloneWindows64,
            options = BuildOptions.StrictMode
        });
        if (report.summary.result != BuildResult.Succeeded)
            throw new Exception("Build failed: " + report.summary.result);
        Directory.CreateDirectory(Path.Combine(sample, "results"));
        // Unity puts recovery/debug material beside the player. Keep it out of the scan target.
        string backup = Path.Combine(output, "CombatBenchmark_BackUpThisFolder_ButDontShipItWithYourGame");
        if (Directory.Exists(backup))
            Directory.Move(backup, Path.Combine(sample, "results", variant + "-backup-" + DateTime.UtcNow.ToString("yyyyMMddHHmmssfff")));
        File.WriteAllText(Path.Combine(sample, "results", variant + "-build.txt"),
            "Unity=" + Application.unityVersion + "\nBackend=" + PlayerSettings.GetScriptingBackend(NamedBuildTarget.Standalone)
            + "\nTarget=StandaloneWindows64\nDevelopment=false\nResult=" + report.summary.result + "\n");
    }
}

```

`benchmarks/unity/combat-formula/src/Assets/Scripts/BenchmarkApp.cs`:

```cs
using System;
using System.IO;
using UnityEngine;
using Reshield.Benchmarks.Combat;

// The player accepts external inputs; expected answers are never embedded in the build.
public sealed class BenchmarkApp : MonoBehaviour
{
    [Serializable] public class InputCase { public string id; public int attack; public int defense; public bool critical; }
    [Serializable] public class InputSet { public InputCase[] cases; }
    [Serializable] public class OutputCase { public string id; public int damage; }
    [Serializable] public class OutputSet { public OutputCase[] results; }

    [RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod(RuntimeInitializeLoadType.AfterSceneLoad)]
    private static void Launch() { new GameObject("Combat benchmark").AddComponent<BenchmarkApp>(); }

    private void Start()
    {
        Debug.Log("Damage example: " + DamageCalculator.Calculate(12, 5, false));
        string[] args = Environment.GetCommandLineArgs();
        int casesIndex = Array.IndexOf(args, "-benchmarkCases");
        if (casesIndex < 0) return;
        try
        {
            int resultIndex = Array.IndexOf(args, "-benchmarkResult");
            if (resultIndex < 0) throw new ArgumentException("-benchmarkResult required");
            InputSet input = JsonUtility.FromJson<InputSet>(File.ReadAllText(args[casesIndex + 1]));
            OutputSet output = new OutputSet { results = new OutputCase[input.cases.Length] };
            for (int i = 0; i < input.cases.Length; i++)
            {
                InputCase c = input.cases[i];
                output.results[i] = new OutputCase { id = c.id, damage = DamageCalculator.Calculate(c.attack, c.defense, c.critical) };
            }
            File.WriteAllText(args[resultIndex + 1], JsonUtility.ToJson(output, true));
            Application.Quit(0);
        }
        catch (Exception e) { Debug.LogException(e); Application.Quit(1); }
    }

    private void OnGUI()
    {
        GUI.Label(new Rect(20, 20, 600, 40), "ReShield combat benchmark: attack=12, defense=5");
        GUI.Label(new Rect(20, 60, 600, 40), "Normal: " + DamageCalculator.Calculate(12, 5, false));
        GUI.Label(new Rect(20, 100, 600, 40), "Critical: " + DamageCalculator.Calculate(12, 5, true));
    }
}

```

`benchmarks/unity/combat-formula/src/Assets/Scripts/DamageCalculator.cs`:

```cs
using System;

namespace Reshield.Benchmarks.Combat
{
    public static class DamageCalculator
    {
        public static int Calculate(int attack, int defense, bool critical)
        {
            if (attack < 0 || attack > 1000000 || defense < 0 || defense > 1000000)
                throw new ArgumentOutOfRangeException("Inputs must be integers in [0, 1000000].");
            int baseDamage = Math.Max(0, attack - defense);
            return critical ? baseDamage * 2 : baseDamage;
        }
    }
}

```

`benchmarks/unity/combat-formula/tests/README.md`:

```md
# Unity tests

**English** | [繁體中文](README.zh-TW.md)

`ground-truth.json` holds the expected behavior. It must be identical to the Godot and Web copies.

From the repository root, run `python benchmarks/run_behavior.py unity mono` or `unity il2cpp`.
The shared runner only passes inputs to the actual Player and compares every output. A non-zero exit code, a timeout, missing output or a different answer all fail.
Results are written to this sample's `results/<variant>-behavior.json`; they are never inferred from file names or mocks.

Check the build output separately with `pwsh -File benchmarks/unity/combat-formula/tests/inspect-mono.ps1`
or `python benchmarks/unity/combat-formula/tests/inspect_il2cpp.py`.
Neither a behavior PASS nor an output-check PASS replaces the production adapter integration check, which is not done yet.

## Static Mono inspection

Requires Python 3.11+ and PowerShell 7.2+ (verified locally with 7.6.5), with no additional third-party packages.
`inspect-mono.ps1` uses `Add-Type` to compile the bundled `StaticMonoInspector.cs` reader.
The reader parses file bytes through .NET's built-in `PEReader` and `MetadataReader`. It never loads the target DLL into the CLR or resolves or loads its dependencies.
Reading the runtime's opcode definitions does not involve reflection on the target DLL.

The original class, method, full IL decoding and `sub` / `Math.Max` / `mul` evidence checks are retained.
Additional checks cover an IL-only managed DLL, the public static `int Calculate(int, int, bool)` and `int Math.Max(int, int)` signatures, and truncated IL.
These are observable IL evidence checks, not a complete proof of the formula's semantics; the nine Player behavior cases remain a separate acceptance check.

Rebuild the DLL with `build.ps1 -Variant mono` as described in the parent README. The reader is compiled automatically in each new PowerShell process and needs no separate build step.
Optional arguments are `-Dll <file>` and `-Report <JSON path>` (the report's parent directory must already exist). The target file is read-only; only the report is written.

```powershell
python -B -m unittest discover -s benchmarks/tests -v
```

Regression tests compile repository-owned fixtures into a temporary directory and check valid IL, missing classes or methods, incorrect signatures, missing formula instructions and invalid PE files.
Before and after inspection, the tests confirm that the target assembly is not loaded and its SHA-256 is unchanged. If an existing Unity DLL is available, it is also copied to a temporary directory without dependency DLLs and inspected there; this test is marked SKIP when no build output is available.

```

`benchmarks/unity/combat-formula/tests/StaticMonoInspector.cs`:

```cs
// Reads target bytes only. Never loads target assemblies or resolves their dependencies.
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.IO;
using System.Linq;
using System.Reflection;
using System.Reflection.Emit;
using System.Reflection.Metadata;
using System.Reflection.Metadata.Ecma335;
using System.Reflection.PortableExecutable;

namespace Reshield.Benchmarks
{
    public static class StaticMonoInspector
    {
        private static string TypeName(MetadataReader reader, EntityHandle handle)
        {
            if (handle.Kind == HandleKind.TypeReference)
            {
                var type = reader.GetTypeReference((TypeReferenceHandle)handle);
                return reader.GetString(type.Namespace) + "." + reader.GetString(type.Name);
            }
            if (handle.Kind == HandleKind.TypeDefinition)
            {
                var type = reader.GetTypeDefinition((TypeDefinitionHandle)handle);
                return reader.GetString(type.Namespace) + "." + reader.GetString(type.Name);
            }
            throw new BadImageFormatException("Unsupported declaring type: " + handle.Kind);
        }

        private static string MethodName(MetadataReader reader, int token)
        {
            var handle = MetadataTokens.EntityHandle(token);
            string name, owner;
            BlobHandle signature;
            if (handle.Kind == HandleKind.MemberReference)
            {
                var member = reader.GetMemberReference((MemberReferenceHandle)handle);
                if (member.GetKind() != MemberReferenceKind.Method)
                    throw new BadImageFormatException("Method operand refers to a field");
                name = reader.GetString(member.Name);
                owner = TypeName(reader, member.Parent);
                signature = member.Signature;
            }
            else if (handle.Kind == HandleKind.MethodDefinition)
            {
                var method = reader.GetMethodDefinition((MethodDefinitionHandle)handle);
                name = reader.GetString(method.Name);
                owner = TypeName(reader, method.GetDeclaringType());
                signature = method.Signature;
            }
            else throw new BadImageFormatException("Unsupported method token: " + handle.Kind);
            if (owner == "System.Math" && name == "Max" &&
                !reader.GetBlobBytes(signature).SequenceEqual(new byte[] { 0, 2, 8, 8, 8 }))
                throw new BadImageFormatException("Expected Int32 Math.Max(Int32, Int32)");
            return owner + "." + name;
        }

        public static string[] Inspect(byte[] image)
        {
            using (var stream = new MemoryStream(image, false))
            using (var pe = new PEReader(stream))
            {
                if (!pe.HasMetadata || pe.PEHeaders.CorHeader == null ||
                    (pe.PEHeaders.CoffHeader.Characteristics & Characteristics.Dll) == 0 ||
                    (pe.PEHeaders.CorHeader.Flags & CorFlags.ILOnly) == 0)
                    throw new BadImageFormatException("Expected an IL-only managed DLL");
                var reader = pe.GetMetadataReader();
                if (!reader.IsAssembly) throw new BadImageFormatException("Expected assembly metadata");
                var types = reader.TypeDefinitions.Where(h =>
                    TypeName(reader, h) == "Reshield.Benchmarks.Combat.DamageCalculator").ToArray();
                if (types.Length != 1) throw new BadImageFormatException("Expected one DamageCalculator type");
                var methods = reader.GetTypeDefinition(types[0]).GetMethods().Where(h =>
                    reader.GetString(reader.GetMethodDefinition(h).Name) == "Calculate").ToArray();
                if (methods.Length != 1) throw new BadImageFormatException("Expected one Calculate method");
                var target = reader.GetMethodDefinition(methods[0]);
                if ((target.Attributes & MethodAttributes.Public) == 0 ||
                    (target.Attributes & MethodAttributes.Static) == 0 ||
                    !reader.GetBlobBytes(target.Signature).SequenceEqual(new byte[] { 0, 3, 8, 8, 8, 2 }) ||
                    target.RelativeVirtualAddress == 0)
                    throw new BadImageFormatException("Expected public static Int32 Calculate(Int32, Int32, Boolean) with IL");
                var bytes = pe.GetMethodBody(target.RelativeVirtualAddress).GetILBytes();
                // Reflection reads the runtime opcode catalog only, never the target DLL.
                var opcodes = typeof(OpCodes).GetFields().Where(f => f.FieldType == typeof(OpCode))
                    .Select(f => (OpCode)f.GetValue(null)).ToDictionary(op => unchecked((ushort)op.Value));
                var instructions = new List<string>();
                int position = 0;
                while (position < bytes.Length)
                {
                    int code = bytes[position++];
                    if (code == 254)
                    {
                        if (position >= bytes.Length) throw new BadImageFormatException("Truncated opcode");
                        code = 65280 + bytes[position++];
                    }
                    if (!opcodes.TryGetValue((ushort)code, out var op))
                        throw new BadImageFormatException("Unknown IL opcode");
                    int size;
                    switch (op.OperandType)
                    {
                        case OperandType.InlineNone: size = 0; break;
                        case OperandType.ShortInlineBrTarget:
                        case OperandType.ShortInlineI:
                        case OperandType.ShortInlineVar: size = 1; break;
                        case OperandType.InlineVar: size = 2; break;
                        case OperandType.InlineI8:
                        case OperandType.InlineR: size = 8; break;
                        case OperandType.InlineSwitch:
                            throw new BadImageFormatException("Unexpected switch in this minimal method");
                        default: size = 4; break;
                    }
                    if (size > bytes.Length - position) throw new BadImageFormatException("Truncated IL operand");
                    string operand = op.OperandType == OperandType.InlineMethod
                        ? MethodName(reader, BitConverter.ToInt32(bytes, position)) : "";
                    instructions.Add((op.Name + " " + operand).Trim());
                    position += size;
                }
                if (!instructions.Contains("sub") || !instructions.Contains("mul") ||
                    !instructions.Contains("call System.Math.Max"))
                    throw new BadImageFormatException("Expected damage formula IL (sub, Math.Max, mul) not found");
                return instructions.ToArray();
            }
        }
    }
}

```

`benchmarks/unity/combat-formula/tests/inspect-mono.ps1`:

```ps1
#requires -Version 7.2
[CmdletBinding()]
param(
    [string]$Dll = (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot '../builds/mono/CombatBenchmark_Data/Managed/Assembly-CSharp.dll'),
    [string]$Report = (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot '../results/mono-inspection.json')
)
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
# Compile only the repository-owned reader, never the inspected assembly.
if (-not ('Reshield.Benchmarks.StaticMonoInspector' -as [type])) {
    Add-Type -Path (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'StaticMonoInspector.cs')
}
$bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes((Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $Dll).Path)
$instructions = [Reshield.Benchmarks.StaticMonoInspector]::Inspect($bytes)
$result = [ordered]@{
    status = 'PASS'
    analysis = 'static PE/CLI metadata and IL; target assembly is not loaded'
    type = 'Reshield.Benchmarks.Combat.DamageCalculator'
    method = 'Calculate'
    sha256 = [Convert]::ToHexString([System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::HashData($bytes))
    instructions = @($instructions)
}
$json = $result | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 4
$json | Set-Content -LiteralPath $Report -Encoding utf8
$json

```

`benchmarks/unity/combat-formula/tests/inspect_il2cpp.py`:

```py
"""Assert artifact evidence for our Windows x64 IL2CPP benchmark, without running it."""
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path
import struct

sample = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
build = sample / "builds/il2cpp"
native = build / "GameAssembly.dll"
metadata = build / "CombatBenchmark_Data/il2cpp_data/Metadata/global-metadata.dat"
data = native.read_bytes()
assert data[:2] == b"MZ"
pe = struct.unpack_from("<I", data, 0x3C)[0]
assert data[pe:pe + 4] == b"PE\0\0"
assert struct.unpack_from("<H", data, pe + 4)[0] == 0x8664, "Expected AMD64"
optional = pe + 24
assert struct.unpack_from("<H", data, optional)[0] == 0x20B, "Expected PE32+"
clr_rva, clr_size = struct.unpack_from("<II", data, optional + 112 + 14 * 8)
assert (clr_rva, clr_size) == (0, 0), "GameAssembly should be native, not managed IL"
raw = metadata.read_bytes()
magic, version = struct.unpack_from("<II", raw)
assert magic == 0xFAB11BAF, "Expected readable IL2CPP metadata header"
for name in (b"DamageCalculator\0", b"Calculate\0", b"Reshield.Benchmarks.Combat\0"):
    assert name in raw, f"Missing readable metadata name: {name}"
assert not list(build.rglob("Assembly-CSharp.dll")), "Managed game assembly accidentally shipped"
assert not list(build.glob("*_BackUpThisFolder_ButDontShipItWithYourGame")), "Do not scan/publish Unity's backup directory"
record = (sample / "results/il2cpp-build.txt").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
assert "Backend=IL2CPP" in record and "Development=false" in record
report = {"status": "PASS", "variant": "il2cpp", "metadata_version": version,
          "evidence": ["Native AMD64 GameAssembly.dll without CLR directory", "Readable IL2CPP metadata type/method/namespace names", "No Assembly-CSharp.dll shipped"],
          "sha256": {p.relative_to(build).as_posix(): hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in (native, metadata)}}
(sample / "results/il2cpp-inspection.json").write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
print(json.dumps(report, indent=2))

```

`benchmarks/web/combat-formula/README.md`:

```md
# Web combat-formula

**English** | [繁體中文](README.zh-TW.md)

A minimal HTML and JavaScript site with no framework. Enter attack, defense and critical hit, then press "Calculate" to get the result.
Tested with Node.js **24.18.0** and npm **11.16.0**. The minifier is **esbuild 0.25.12**, pinned through the lockfile.
L0 needs no npm packages; only the two minified variants need the local project dependencies.

## Three variants

| Variant | Output | Conditions | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| plain | `builds/plain/` | Original JS copied verbatim, not minified | L0 |
| minified | `builds/minified/` | esbuild bundle and minify, no map; readable properties and strings remain | L1 |
| minified-map | `builds/minified-map/` | Same minification, but ships a linked map with `sourcesContent` | L0 |

Each of the two JS entry points is bundled as an IIFE. There is no property mangling or string encryption, so L2 is not claimed.
Settings follow the [esbuild API documentation](https://esbuild.github.io/api/).

## Build and test (from the repository root)

Do L0 first:

```powershell
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/test.mjs source
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/build.mjs plain
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/test.mjs plain
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/inspect.mjs plain
```

After L0 is done on all three platforms, install the tools **locally to the project**, with no global installs:

```powershell
Push-Location benchmarks/web/combat-formula/src
npm.cmd ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund --cache ../../../.tools/npm-cache
Pop-Location
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/build.mjs minified
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/build.mjs minified-map
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/test.mjs minified
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/test.mjs minified-map
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/inspect.mjs minified
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/inspect.mjs minified-map
```

With `--ignore-scripts`, npm's optional platform dependency provides the esbuild binary. Do not use `--omit=optional`.
If the system blocks `npm.ps1`, use `npm.cmd` as above; there is no need to change the PowerShell execution policy.

## Run in a browser and check by hand

Open `builds/<variant>/index.html` directly, or start a server reachable only from this machine:

```powershell
python -m http.server 8765 --bind 127.0.0.1 --directory benchmarks/web/combat-formula/builds
```

Open `http://127.0.0.1:8765/plain/`, `/minified/` and `/minified-map/`.
Enter each case from `tests/ground-truth.json`, press "Calculate" and check the output.
In DevTools → Sources: plain shows the formula directly, minified has no source map, and minified-map shows the original files through `sourcesContent`.
Press Ctrl+C in the server terminal when done.

## What the automated checks cover

- `test.mjs` loads **the given source or shipped JS** in a Node VM and runs the same 9 cases. It does not reimplement the formula.
- `inspect.mjs` restricts the shipped file list and checks the HTML references; plain must equal the source.
- Variants without a map must have no `.map`, inline map or `sourceMappingURL`, and the minified output must still contain `calculateDamage` and the diagnostic strings.
- The map variant is decoded with Node's `SourceMap` to verify that the generated `Math.max` maps back to the correct source line and column, and `sourcesContent` is checked.
- Browser interaction is a separate acceptance layer. For the latest results of the 9 cases on all three variants, see the [RESULTS.md](../../RESULTS.md).

Ground truth lives in `tests/`, and the build only ships allow-listed files. Only the map variant leaks the original source, on purpose, as a risk comparison.
`reshield.toml` sets the target to L2; `expected.toml` records the expected L0 or L1 and does not assume the production adapter can already detect or link assets.
`builds/`, `results/` and `node_modules/` are not committed; `package-lock.json` is kept.

```

`benchmarks/web/combat-formula/README.zh-TW.md`:

```md
# Web combat-formula

[English](README.md) | **繁體中文**

HTML＋JavaScript 最小網站，不需框架。輸入攻擊、防禦與暴擊，按「計算」取得結果。
測試環境：Node.js **24.18.0**、npm **11.16.0**；壓縮工具 **esbuild 0.25.12**，依賴以 lockfile 固定。
L0 不需要安裝 npm 套件；只有兩個壓縮變體需要本機專案依賴。

## 三個變體

| 變體 | 發布位置 | 條件 | 預期 |
|---|---|---|---|
| plain | `builds/plain/` | 原始 JS 逐字複製，不壓縮 | L0 |
| minified | `builds/minified/` | esbuild bundle＋minify，無 map，仍保留可讀屬性與字串 | L1 |
| minified-map | `builds/minified-map/` | 相同壓縮設定，但發布 linked map 與 sourcesContent | L0 |

兩個 JS 入口各自以 IIFE 格式打包。沒有 property mangling 或字串加密，不宣稱 L2。
設定依 [esbuild 官方 API](https://esbuild.github.io/api/)。

## 建置與測試（Repository 根目錄）

先完成 L0：

```powershell
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/test.mjs source
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/build.mjs plain
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/test.mjs plain
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/inspect.mjs plain
```

三平台 L0 完成後，安裝**專案本地**工具，無全域安裝：

```powershell
Push-Location benchmarks/web/combat-formula/src
npm.cmd ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund --cache ../../../.tools/npm-cache
Pop-Location
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/build.mjs minified
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/build.mjs minified-map
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/test.mjs minified
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/test.mjs minified-map
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/inspect.mjs minified
node benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/inspect.mjs minified-map
```

`--ignore-scripts` 搭配 npm optional platform dependency 提供 esbuild binary；不要用 `--omit=optional`。
若系統阻止 npm.ps1，使用上述 npm.cmd，無需修改 PowerShell execution policy。

## 瀏覽器執行與手動驗收

直接開 `builds/<variant>/index.html` 即可使用；也可啟動僅本機可讀的伺服器：

```powershell
python -m http.server 8765 --bind 127.0.0.1 --directory benchmarks/web/combat-formula/builds
```

打開 `http://127.0.0.1:8765/plain/`、`/minified/`、`/minified-map/`。
逐一輸入 `tests/ground-truth.json` 案例，按「計算」，核對 output。
DevTools → Sources：plain 可直接讀公式；minified 沒有來源 map；minified-map 能看到 `sourcesContent` 的原始檔。
驗證後在伺服器終端按 Ctrl+C。

## 自動檢查的範圍

- `test.mjs` 在 Node VM 載入**指定來源或發布 JS**，使用相同 9 案例測試；不是重寫另一份公式當功能實作。
- `inspect.mjs` 限制發布檔案清單、檢查 HTML 引用，plain 必須等於來源。
- 無 map 版本不可有 `.map`、inline map 或 `sourceMappingURL`；壓縮後仍需保留 `calculateDamage` 和診斷字串。
- 有 map 版本使用 Node `SourceMap` 真正解碼映射，驗證 generated `Math.max` 對回來源的正確行列，並核對 sourcesContent。
- 瀏覽器互動是另一層驗收；最近一次三變體的 9 案例實測結果見 [RESULTS.md](../../RESULTS.md)。

Ground Truth 放在 `tests/`，建置只發布白名單檔案。只有 map 變體刻意洩漏原始碼，作為風險對照。
`reshield.toml` 設定目標 L2；`expected.toml` 記錄預期 L0／L1，不預設正式 Adapter 已能辨識或關聯資產。
`builds/`、`results/`、`node_modules/` 不提交；保留 package-lock.json。

```

`benchmarks/web/combat-formula/build.mjs`:

```mjs
import { mkdir, copyFile, readFile, writeFile, readdir, unlink } from "node:fs/promises";
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import path from "node:path";

const root = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
const variant = process.argv[2] ?? "plain";
if (!["plain", "minified", "minified-map"].includes(variant)) throw new Error("Unknown variant: " + variant);
const output = path.join(root, "builds", variant);
await mkdir(output, { recursive: true });
// Only replace known generated files; refuse unexpected content rather than deleting it.
const generated = new Set(["index.html", "combat.js", "app.js", "combat.js.map", "app.js.map"]);
for (const name of await readdir(output)) {
  if (!generated.has(name)) throw new Error("Unexpected output file: " + name);
  await unlink(path.join(output, name));
}
await copyFile(path.join(root, "src/index.html"), path.join(output, "index.html"));
if (variant === "plain") {
  for (const name of ["combat.js", "app.js"])
    await copyFile(path.join(root, "src", name), path.join(output, name));
} else {
  const require = createRequire(path.join(root, "src/package.json"));
  const esbuild = require("esbuild");
  for (const name of ["combat.js", "app.js"]) {
    await esbuild.build({
      entryPoints: [path.join(root, "src", name)], outfile: path.join(output, name),
      bundle: true, minify: true, format: "iife", platform: "browser", target: "es2020",
      sourcemap: variant === "minified-map" ? "linked" : false,
      sourcesContent: true, legalComments: "none"
    });
  }
}
await mkdir(path.join(root, "results"), { recursive: true });
await writeFile(path.join(root, "results", variant + "-build.json"), JSON.stringify({
  node: process.version, variant, minified: variant !== "plain", sourceMap: variant === "minified-map",
  esbuild: variant === "plain" ? null : JSON.parse(await readFile(path.join(root, "src/node_modules/esbuild/package.json"), "utf8")).version
}, null, 2));
console.log("PASS build " + variant + ": " + output);

```

`benchmarks/web/combat-formula/reshield.toml`:

[project]
name = "ReShield web combat benchmark"

[[asset]]
id = "combat-formulas"
description = "Damage after defense, doubled for critical hits"
value = "high"
paths = ["src/combat.js"]
symbols = ["ReshieldCombat.*"]
target_level = "L2"
performance_budget = "low"
server_side = false

```

`benchmarks/web/combat-formula/src/app.js`:

```js
const form = document.getElementById("combat-form");
function updateDamage(event) {
  if (event) event.preventDefault();
  const attack = Number(document.getElementById("attack").value);
  const defense = Number(document.getElementById("defense").value);
  const critical = document.getElementById("critical").checked;
  document.getElementById("damage").textContent = String(
    globalThis.ReshieldCombat.calculateDamage(attack, defense, critical)
  );
}
form.addEventListener("submit", updateDamage);
updateDamage();

```

`benchmarks/web/combat-formula/src/combat.js`:

```js
// Original ReShield benchmark. Domain: integer attack/defense in [0, 1000000].
globalThis.ReshieldCombat = {
  calculateDamage(attack, defense, critical) {
    if (!Number.isInteger(attack) || !Number.isInteger(defense) ||
        attack < 0 || defense < 0 || attack > 1000000 || defense > 1000000 ||
        typeof critical !== "boolean") {
      throw new TypeError("Expected integers in [0, 1000000] and a boolean critical flag.");
    }
    const baseDamage = Math.max(0, attack - defense);
    return critical ? baseDamage * 2 : baseDamage;
  }
};

```

`benchmarks/web/combat-formula/src/index.html`:

```html
<!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
<meta charset="utf-8">
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width,initial-scale=1">
<title>ReShield Damage Calculation Benchmark</title>
<body>
<h1>ReShield Damage Calculation Benchmark</h1>
<form id="combat-form">
  <p><label>Attack <input id="attack" type="number" min="0" max="1000000" step="1" value="12" required></label></p>
  <p><label>Defense <input id="defense" type="number" min="0" max="1000000" step="1" value="5" required></label></p>
  <p><label><input id="critical" type="checkbox"> Critical hit</label></p>
  <button type="submit">Calculate</button>
</form>
<p>Damage: <output id="damage" aria-live="polite"></output></p>
<script src="combat.js"></script>
<script src="app.js"></script>
</body>
</html>

```

`benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/inspect.mjs`:

```mjs
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { readFile, readdir, mkdir, writeFile } from "node:fs/promises";
import { createHash } from "node:crypto";
import { SourceMap } from "node:module";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

const root = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
const variant = process.argv[2];
assert(["plain", "minified", "minified-map"].includes(variant), "Unknown variant");
const output = path.join(root, "builds", variant);
const names = (await readdir(output)).sort();
const expectedFiles = ["app.js", "combat.js", "index.html"];
if (variant === "minified-map") expectedFiles.push("app.js.map", "combat.js.map");
assert.deepEqual(names, expectedFiles.sort(), "Unexpected files may leak original code or answers");
const html = await readFile(path.join(output, "index.html"), "utf8");
assert(html.includes('src="combat.js"') && html.includes('src="app.js"'));
const evidence = [];
const hashes = {};
for (const name of ["combat.js", "app.js"]) {
  const original = await readFile(path.join(root, "src", name), "utf8");
  const generated = await readFile(path.join(output, name), "utf8");
  hashes[name] = createHash("sha256").update(generated).digest("hex");
  if (variant === "plain") {
    assert.equal(generated, original);
    evidence.push(`${name}: exact unminified source`);
  } else {
    const code = generated.replace(/\/\/# sourceMappingURL=.*\n?$/, "");
    assert(code.length < original.length, "Expected smaller minified output");
    assert(code.trim().split("\n").length === 1, "Expected single-line minified output");
    evidence.push(`${name}: smaller single-line bundled/minified output`);
  }
  if (variant !== "minified-map") {
    assert(!generated.includes("sourceMappingURL"), "Unexpected external or inline map");
    evidence.push(`${name}: no external or inline source map`);
  } else {
    assert(generated.includes(`//# sourceMappingURL=${name}.map`));
    const payload = JSON.parse(await readFile(path.join(output, name + ".map"), "utf8"));
    assert.equal(payload.version, 3);
    assert.deepEqual(payload.sources, [`../../src/${name}`]);
    assert.deepEqual(payload.sourcesContent, [original]);
    assert(payload.mappings.length > 0);
    // Decode an actual generated position, rather than accepting a nonempty .map file.
    const needle = name === "combat.js" ? "Math.max" : "getElementById";
    const column = generated.indexOf(needle);
    assert(column >= 0);
    const entry = new SourceMap(payload).findEntry(0, column);
    assert.equal(entry.originalSource, `../../src/${name}`);
    assert.equal(entry.originalLine, original.split("\n").findIndex(line => line.includes(needle)));
    assert.equal(original.split("\n")[entry.originalLine].slice(entry.originalColumn, entry.originalColumn + needle.length), needle);
    evidence.push(`${name}: generated 1:${column + 1} maps to source ${entry.originalLine + 1}:${entry.originalColumn + 1}`);
  }
}
const combat = await readFile(path.join(output, "combat.js"), "utf8");
assert(combat.includes("calculateDamage") && combat.includes("Expected integers"), "L1 fixture should keep readable property/strings");
const report = { status: "PASS", variant, hashes, evidence };
await mkdir(path.join(root, "results"), { recursive: true });
await writeFile(path.join(root, "results", variant + "-inspection.json"), JSON.stringify(report, null, 2));
console.log(JSON.stringify(report, null, 2));

```

`benchmarks/web/combat-formula/tests/test.mjs`:

```mjs
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { readFile } from "node:fs/promises";
import vm from "node:vm";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

const sample = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
const variant = process.argv[2] ?? "source";
const directory = variant === "source" ? path.join(sample, "src") : path.join(sample, "builds", variant);
const truth = JSON.parse(await readFile(path.join(sample, "tests", "ground-truth.json"), "utf8"));
const context = vm.createContext({});
vm.runInContext(await readFile(path.join(directory, "combat.js"), "utf8"), context);
for (const c of truth.cases)
  assert.equal(context.ReshieldCombat.calculateDamage(c.attack, c.defense, c.critical), c.expected, c.id);
assert.throws(() => context.ReshieldCombat.calculateDamage(-1, 0, false));
console.log(`PASS ${variant}: ${truth.cases.length} ground-truth cases + invalid input rejection`);

```

# Example asset declaration. Copy to your project root as reshield.toml.
# Spec: protocol/01-asset-declaration.md

[project]
name = "Example Game"

[[asset]]
id = "combat-formulas"
description = "Damage, combo and balance calculations"
value = "critical"
paths = ["Assets/Scripts/Combat/**"]
symbols = ["ExampleGame.Combat.*"]
target_level = "L3"
performance_budget = "moderate"

[[asset]]
id = "save-format"
description = "Save file structure and validation"
value = "medium"
paths = ["Assets/Scripts/Save/**"]
symbols = ["ExampleGame.Save.*"]
target_level = "L2"
performance_budget = "low"

[[asset]]
id = "economy"
description = "Shop prices and reward rates, computed on the server"
value = "high"
symbols = ["ExampleGame.Economy.*"]
server_side = true

`protocol/01-asset-declaration.md`:

```md
# 01 · Asset declaration

**Status:** v0.1 (2026-11-01).

Developers declare what needs protecting in a `reshield.toml` file at the project root. ReShield looks for it in the scan target and its parent directories, stopping at the repository root (the first directory containing `.git`), or it can be passed with `--manifest`. Reports show which manifest was used. See [`examples/reshield.toml`](../examples/reshield.toml).

## Format

```toml
[project]
name = "My Game"

[[asset]]
id = "combat-formulas"                     # required, unique, kebab-case
description = "Damage and combo calculations"
value = "critical"                         # critical | high | medium | low  (default: medium)
paths = ["Assets/Scripts/Combat/**"]       # source globs, relative to the project root
symbols = ["MyGame.Combat.*"]              # names as they appear in the build
target_level = "L3"                        # L0..L5  (default: depends on value)
performance_budget = "moderate"            # none | low | moderate | high  (default: low)
server_side = false                        # true if this logic runs on a server  (default: false)
```

## Fields

| Field | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | yes | Unique kebab-case id. Findings refer to assets by this id. |
| `description` | no | What the asset is, in plain language. |
| `value` | no | How much damage a leak would cause. Drives the default target level. |
| `paths` | one of `paths` / `symbols` | Source-file globs. Used when reviewing source code and when the build keeps source paths. |
| `symbols` | one of `paths` / `symbols` | Name globs as they appear in the build: C# namespaces or types, Godot resource paths, JS module paths. Adapters use these to link findings to assets. |
| `target_level` | no | The level the developer wants this asset to reach. |
| `performance_budget` | no | How much runtime cost the developer accepts for protecting this asset. Limits which fixes ReShield recommends. |
| `server_side` | no | Declares that the logic runs on a server. The asset counts as L5 (confidence `declared`) unless the scan finds it in the build. |

## Pattern syntax

| Field | `*` | `**` | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `paths` | Any characters within one folder | Any number of folders | `src/Combat/*` matches `src/Combat/Damage.cs` but not `src/Combat/Sub/Damage.cs`; `src/Combat/**` matches both |
| `symbols` | Any characters, including `.` and `/` | Same as `*` | `MyGame.Combat.*` matches every type under the `MyGame.Combat` namespace |

`?` matches one character (within one folder for `paths`). Matching is case-sensitive.

## Defaults

When `target_level` is omitted:

| `value` | Default target |
|---|---|
| critical | L3 |
| high | L2 |
| medium | L2 |
| low | L1 |
| (any, with `server_side = true`) | L5 |

Critical assets default to L3 rather than L4 because virtualization has a high runtime cost; developers opt in to L4 explicitly.

## Linking findings to assets

Adapters MUST try to link each finding to an asset with `Manifest.match_asset(symbol=..., path=...)`. The first asset whose `symbols` or `paths` match wins, so more specific assets SHOULD be declared first.

A finding that matches no asset is still reported, with `asset_id` left empty.

## Validation

ReShield rejects a manifest that has an invalid TOML syntax, a duplicate or non-kebab-case `id`, an unknown `value`, `target_level` or `performance_budget`, or an asset with neither `paths` nor `symbols`.

## After v0.1

- Declaring assets with code annotations (for example a C# attribute) instead of, or in addition to, the manifest
- Per-platform `symbols` when one asset is shipped on several platforms

```

`protocol/02-protection-levels.md`:

```md
# 02 · Protection levels

**Status:** v0.1 (2026-11-01).

## Definitions

| Level | Definition | What an analyst recovers |
|---|---|---|
| L0 | Shipped as-is. Code decompiles back to something close to the original source. | Source-like code with original names |
| L1 | Compiled to native code, but symbols and metadata still expose names and structure. | Structure and names, in a lower-level form |
| L2 | Symbols and metadata are stripped or encrypted. Logic is readable, but its intent is not. | Logic without meaningful names |
| L3 | Control flow and data are transformed. Recovering logic requires dedicated deobfuscation. | Logic only after significant extra work |
| L4 | Critical functions are virtualized. An attacker must first reverse a custom VM. | Logic only after reversing the VM itself |
| L5 | The asset never ships. The logic runs on a server the attacker cannot access. | Only inputs and outputs, never the logic |

## Requirements

- A level MUST mean the same degree of protection on every platform. The test is the right-hand column above: a Unity L2 and a Web L2 must leave an analyst with comparable information.
- A level applies to an **asset**, not to a whole build. Different assets in one build can sit at different levels.
- An adapter MUST report the level it actually found, not the level the developer intended.
- A level is reached only when **all** of its conditions hold. If any L2 condition fails, the asset is at L1 at most.
- A level predicted from a source project or build settings, without a build, is at most `inferred`. Settings can be overridden at build time, so only the build can make it `observed`.
- Criteria describe what an analyst can recover, not a file-format version. Adapters SHOULD decide a level from observations that hold across format versions, for example whether names are stored as plain text, and use version-specific parsing only to add detail such as method-level locations. An unrecognized format version alone MUST NOT make a result `open` when a version-independent observation is conclusive.
- The overall level of a build is the lowest level of any declared asset. If no assets are declared, it is the lowest level of any `fail` or `pass` result. When the assessment is incomplete, the overall level is only an upper bound (see [05 · Completeness and scoring](05-report-format.md#completeness-and-scoring)).

The numeric target-attainment score is separate from the overall protection level; see [05 · Numeric score](05-report-format.md#numeric-score-asset-target-v1). A high attainment score does not raise the weakest protection level.

## Observability

Not every level can be confirmed by looking at a build. Each criterion below is tagged with the confidence an adapter can claim (see [05 · Report format](05-report-format.md#confidence)):

| Tag | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **O** | Observed: can be checked directly in the build |
| **I** | Inferred: can only be estimated with heuristics |
| **D** | Declared: must come from `reshield.toml`; cannot be seen in the build |

As a rule, L0–L2 are observable, L3–L4 are inferred, and L5 is declared. An adapter MUST NOT claim a level higher than it can support with the matching confidence.

## Per-platform criteria

These are the conditions that place an asset at each level. Adapters implement them; owners keep them up to date as adapters learn more.

### Unity

| Level | Condition | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| L0 | Scripting backend is **Mono**: game code ships as managed assemblies (for example `Assembly-CSharp.dll` under `<Product>_Data/Managed/`) | O |
| L1 | Scripting backend is **IL2CPP**, but the IL2CPP metadata file ships readable, so type and method names can still be recovered | O |
| L2 | IL2CPP, **and** the metadata is encrypted or its names are obfuscated, **and** no debug symbols or development-build flags ship | O |
| L3 | L2, **and** the asset's code shows control-flow or data transformation | I |
| L4 | L2, **and** the asset's code is virtualized | I |
| L5 | The asset is declared server-side in `reshield.toml` | D |

### Godot

| Level | Condition | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| L0 | GDScript is exported as **text**, or C# code ships as managed assemblies, inside an **unencrypted** PCK | O |
| L1 | GDScript is exported as **binary tokens**, but the PCK is unencrypted | O |
| L2 | The PCK (or the embedded PCK) is **encrypted**, **and** C# assemblies, if any, are name-obfuscated | O |
| L3 | L2, **and** script or native code shows control-flow or data transformation | I |
| L4 | L2, **and** the asset's logic lives in virtualized native code (GDExtension) | I |
| L5 | The asset is declared server-side in `reshield.toml` | D |

Note: Godot's PCK encryption key is compiled into the export template, so encryption raises cost but does not reach L3 on its own.

### Web / Electron

| Level | Condition | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| L0 | JavaScript ships **unminified**, or **source maps** ship (as `.map` files or inline), or Electron app code ships as plain files or in an unprotected `app.asar` | O |
| L1 | JavaScript is **bundled and minified**, no source maps ship, but property names, strings and module structure remain readable | O |
| L2 | L1, **and** identifiers and property names are mangled, **and** sensitive strings are not stored in plain text | O |
| L3 | L2, **and** the asset's code shows control-flow or data transformation | I |
| L4 | L2, **and** the asset's logic runs inside a virtualized interpreter, or in WebAssembly that is itself at L3 or above | I |
| L5 | The asset is declared server-side in `reshield.toml` | D |

Note: `app.asar` is an archive format, not a protection. Putting code in it does not raise the level.

### Native and .NET programs

The fallback for any program not built with Unity, Godot or Web technology: C, C++, Rust, Go, Swift and Objective-C binaries (PE, ELF, Mach-O) and .NET assemblies.

| Level | Condition | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| L0 | A .NET assembly of the developer's own code ships with readable IL (framework assemblies are excluded) | O |
| L1 | A native binary ships a symbol table, debug information (DWARF), C++ class names in RTTI, Go function tables, or Objective-C/Swift class names | O |
| L2 | None of the L1 markers were found. Machine code and text strings remain readable | I |
| L3 | Not checked | — |
| L4 | Not checked | — |
| L5 | The asset is declared server-side in `reshield.toml` | D |

C++ class names are detected for MSVC (`.?AV`) and for GCC and Clang (Itanium mangled names, at least 5 that are not from the standard library).

### Android Java and Kotlin

| Level | Condition | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| L0 | The app's own DEX classes keep readable names (library and build-generated classes excluded) | O |
| L2 | At least half of the app's classes have renamed names of 1 or 2 characters (R8 or ProGuard) | I |

### Unreal Engine content

| Level | Condition | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | A `.pak` or IoStore `.utoc` container's index is not encrypted | O |
| L2 | The container's index is encrypted. The key ships in the game executable | I |

Unreal game code itself is C++ and is checked by the native adapter, which runs together with this one.

### Packaged builds

`.apk`, `.aab`, `.ipa`, `.zip`, `.xapk` and `.apks` files are opened read-only into a private temporary copy. Every adapter that recognizes the contents runs, and locations point inside the package, for example `game.apk!/lib/arm64-v8a/libil2cpp.so`. Unity Android and iOS layouts are recognized inside packages.

## Cross-platform findings

Some findings are not about levels and apply to every platform. They use `RS-CORE-NNN` rule ids and are reported at the level of the asset they expose:

| Rule | Finding | Level | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `RS-CORE-001` | Credentials embedded in the build: API keys, tokens, private keys, key stores | L0 | Implemented (v0.1) |
| `RS-CORE-002` | Debug files shipped: symbol files, Unity do-not-ship folders | L1, or L0 for generated source | Implemented (v0.1) |
| `RS-CORE-004` | Code in a runtime no adapter checks yet (Python, Flutter, React Native, Java, Lua, GameMaker, Ren'Py, Xamarin), reported as `open` | none | Implemented (v0.1) |
| `RS-CORE-005` | A known protection tool was applied (VMProtect, Themida, WinLicense, Enigma, ConfuserEx, Dotfuscator, SmartAssembly, Babel, Obfuscator-LLVM, javascript-obfuscator), reported as `pass` | L2, inferred | Implemented (v0.1) |
| — | Development builds shipped to users | — | Planned |
| — | Server endpoints or internal URLs that reveal back-end structure | — | Planned |

The core runs these checks on every build after the platform adapter. Credential checks use high-precision patterns only and never print a key in full.

## Decisions for v0.1

- Adapters report L0 and L1 only. L2 to L4 are listed as not checked; an unreadable or nonstandard format is `open`, never L2.
- L3 and L4 need a benchmark-calibrated heuristic (see [04](04-ai-resilience-measurement.md)) before any adapter may report them.
- Calibration across Unity, Godot and Web is recorded in [`benchmarks/RESULTS.md`](../benchmarks/RESULTS.md). L0 and L1 are consistent; recovery effort within L1 varies by platform.
- Web JavaScript that is read directly from the build counts as `observed` when every parameter name agrees (all descriptive or all single letters); mixed cases stay `open` (decided 2026-11-02).

## Open for v0.2

- [ ] Detecting encrypted or protected metadata without depending on a specific protection product.
- [ ] Unreal Engine, native C/C++, Android and iOS criteria.

```

`protocol/04-ai-resilience-measurement.md`:

```md
# 04 · AI-resilience measurement

**Status:** v0.1 method (2026-11-01); first AI recovery run 2026-11-02.

Defines a reproducible way to measure how much effort an AI-assisted analyst needs to recover an asset, so that levels and scores can be calibrated against evidence rather than opinion.

## Concepts

- **Reference analyst:** a fixed, versioned configuration (model, tools, prompts). Results are only comparable within the same version.
- **Benchmark samples:** small programs with known behavior, built at several protection levels. See [`benchmarks/`](../benchmarks/).

## Candidate metrics

| Metric | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Semantic recovery rate | Share of the original tests that the recovered code passes |
| Intent identification | Whether the analyst correctly states what the code does |
| Recovery cost | Tokens, time and steps used |

## v0.1

The full method, record format and metrics are in [`benchmarks/measurement/MEASUREMENT.md`](../benchmarks/measurement/MEASUREMENT.md). Build and scan results for all three platforms are in [`benchmarks/RESULTS.md`](../benchmarks/RESULTS.md).

- **In v0.1:** scan success rate, effective analysis rate, evidence completeness, and asset and rule coverage, measured on real builds.
- **First run (2026-11-02):** semantic recovery rate, intent identification and recovery cost on 7 builds; see [`benchmarks/RESULTS.md`](../benchmarks/RESULTS.md#ai-recovery-first-run-2026-11-02).
- **Versioning:** every result records a `reference_analyst_id` (model, tools, prompt hash and budget). Results are compared only within the same id; a new analyst configuration gets a new id and is measured again.

```

`protocol/05-report-format.md`:

```md
# 05 · Report format

**Status:** v0.1 (2026-11-01).

ReShield produces three formats from the same data:

| Format | Audience | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| JSON | The skill | Full data, see [`schemas/finding.schema.json`](schemas/finding.schema.json). `summary.complete`, `summary.not_checked_levels` and each asset's `status` report coverage |
| SARIF 2.1.0 | GitHub, VS Code, CI | `kind` maps as below. Non-`fail` results use `level: "none"` (§3.27.10). `error` results also appear in `invocations[0].toolExecutionNotifications`, with `executionSuccessful: false`. ReShield fields go in `properties` with the `reshield.` prefix. Each rule is tagged with its [OWASP MASWE](https://mas.owasp.org/MASWE/) weakness, for example `MASWE-0059` |
| Markdown | People | Generated from the same data |

## Required fields per finding

Every finding MUST include: rule id, title, platform, kind, severity, level, confidence, location, impact. A `fail` finding SHOULD include evidence and at least one remediation.

## Result kinds

Each finding is the result of evaluating one rule. Its `kind` says what the evaluation established. The values come from SARIF 2.1.0 and map onto XCCDF 1.2 (NIST IR 7275), the result vocabulary used by configuration-compliance tools such as OpenSCAP:

| ReShield | Meaning | SARIF `kind` | XCCDF result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fail` | Exposure found | `fail` | `fail` |
| `pass` | Protection conditions confirmed | `pass` | `pass` |
| `open` | Not enough information to decide | `open` | `unknown` |
| `error` | Evaluation could not complete | `open`, plus a tool execution notification | `error` |
| `notapplicable` | Rule does not apply | `notApplicable` | `notapplicable` |
| (level not evaluated by the adapter) | Listed in `not_checked_levels`, no result | — | `notchecked` |

ReShield keeps `open` and `error` apart, as XCCDF does, because the developer acts on them differently: `open` needs a newer ReShield or more information, `error` needs the scan environment fixed.

## Completeness and scoring

ReShield follows the fail-safe defaults principle (Saltzer and Schroeder, 1975): missing evidence never counts as protection.

- An assessment is **complete** only when no result is `open` or `error`.
- An asset is **undetermined** when an `open` or `error` result applies to it, either directly or through a whole-build result. Its observed level is empty.
- A level declared in `reshield.toml` (for example L5 for `server_side` assets) counts only when the asset is not undetermined.
- When an assessment is incomplete, the overall level is an **upper bound** and reports MUST say so. No score is given.
- `fail` results outweigh `pass` results for the same asset.

## Numeric score: asset-target-v1

`summary.score` is the integer percentage of declared asset value weight whose targets are attained (policy `asset-target-v1`). Markdown names the policy explicitly.

Weights are critical=4, high=3, medium=2, low=1. Each declared asset contributes once. An asset earns its weight when its existing `observed_level >= target_level` comparison is true; otherwise it earns zero. The score is `round(100 * attained_weight / total_weight)`, using nearest-integer, ties-to-even rounding. There is no partial credit for level distance and no penalty based on duplicate Finding count. Weights are initial policy priorities, not calibrated business-loss ratios.

A numeric score requires a nonempty declared asset scope, a complete assessment, and an available `observed`-confidence level for every declared asset. Any unknown, inferred-only, or declared-only asset withholds the score (`null`), even when `complete` is true. Do not remove these assets from the denominator or call them failed. `open` and `error` retain the existing incomplete-assessment protection. `notapplicable` does not independently establish a level. Observed evidence at the effective asset level can support scoring even if other evidence at that level is inferred or declared, following existing confidence precedence.

Without assets, the overall-level fallback to fail/pass findings remains available, but no target-attainment score is given. Unassociated findings remain excluded from declared-asset scoring and must stay visible in the report's association warnings. This score describes only the declared asset scope, not all shipped code.

This is target compliance, not absolute protection or measured recovery difficulty: target L0 with observed L0 can score 100 while overall_level remains L0. Keep the overall level, confidence, critical assets below target, and coverage limits visible alongside the percentage. Compare scores only for the same asset scope, values, targets, and policy. No release gate or protection guarantee is implied.

Markdown explains withholding reasons. JSON consumers can use the complete flag and per-asset level/confidence/status fields. SARIF has no numeric score field.

## Confidence

Not every level can be seen in a build. Each finding states how its level was established:

| Value | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `observed` | Seen directly in the build artifact | A file that only exists in unprotected builds is present |
| `inferred` | Concluded from heuristics; may be wrong | Code structure suggests control-flow transformation |
| `declared` | Stated by the developer in `reshield.toml`; cannot be verified from the build | Logic declared as running on a server (L5) |

Reports MUST show the confidence next to the level. For asset-target-v1, confidence controls eligibility rather than a numerical multiplier: only observed asset levels qualify. Inferred and declared levels remain reported with their evidence limitations; neither is silently counted as failure or verified attainment.

## Contextual severity: severity-v1

The core normalizes severity after adapter collection and before summarization and serialization. The score uses asset-target-v1.

Severity describes the potential consequence of implementation exposure in the declared asset context, not a vulnerability/incident assertion or a CVSS score. Confidence, kind, level, target comparison, and score eligibility remain separate. Non-fail results retain info.

Registered rules are RS-UNITY-001 (source-like implementation; unassociated baseline high) and RS-UNITY-002 (readable names/structure; unassociated baseline medium). A registered rule must match its expected platform. Classification comes from exact registered rule meaning, not platform prefixes, filenames, level numbers or free-text impact.

| Exposure class | low asset | medium asset | high asset | critical asset |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| source-like-implementation | low | medium | high | critical |
| readable-names-and-structure | low | low | medium | high |

Valid associated rules use this matrix, intentionally replacing the adapter baseline in either direction. An unassociated result uses the rule baseline. Unknown rules, registered platform mismatches, undeclared asset IDs and invalid internal value types retain the adapter severity with a Markdown explanation. Do not attach them to another asset or silently downgrade an unknown rule. The matrix applies only to these implementation-exposure classes; additional classes require review.

Normalize copied findings without mutating adapter inputs. All formats render the same normalized findings; existing summary severity_counts count only fail results. JSON exposes those counts, while SARIF does not acquire a counts field. Direct adapter calls keep baselines and direct writers render supplied ScanResult values without applying normalization themselves.

The Markdown summary includes a severity policy bullet. Each fail entry includes a Severity basis bullet from its recorded normalization decision, explaining the class/value, unassociated baseline, or fallback. Results without recorded normalization are identified as supplied severities. These explanations are Markdown text, not serialized fields. SARIF maps critical/high→error, medium→warning, low/info→note.

Changing target levels, budgets, server-side flags or confidence must not conceal an exposure by changing its consequence classification. A critical/inferred or critical/declared result is still uncertain and must not be described as observed compromise. The initial matrix is a policy choice, not measured business loss; compare only unchanged asset declarations and policy versions.

## Developer summary

The developer summary is the default view for people who are not security specialists. It answers three questions: is my code at risk, what should I do, and what does it cost. It reorganizes the same results; the technical report stays complete and authoritative.

Produced by `scan --format summary` (Markdown) and the `actions` array in JSON.

**Grouping.** `fail` results that share a fix (the first remediation's summary) form one action. Each action lists the declared assets it protects, the other code areas it covers, the files involved, the cost and the steps. Each `open` or `error` result is its own action.

**Priority.**

| Priority | Rule |
|---|---|
| Must fix | Any grouped result belongs to a critical or high value asset that misses its target. Without a linked asset, the result's severity is critical or high |
| Should fix | Every other `fail` group |
| Could not check | `open` and `error` results |

**Wording.** The summary MUST NOT use protocol terms: no level codes, confidence values, result kinds, rule ids or policy names. Levels are described by what an analyst gets back, for example "can be read almost like your source code" for L0. Unchecked levels are named by the protection they stand for, for example "hidden names" for L2.

**Order.** One-sentence verdict, Must fix, Should fix, Could not check, Not checked yet, then, when no assets are declared, the code areas found so the developer can choose what to protect.

**Code areas.** A code area is a symbol's namespace, or its type for code in the global namespace. Without declared assets, adapters SHOULD report exposure once per code area so the summary can list them.

## Markdown report layout

The Markdown report is for people. Every platform MUST produce the same sections in this order, and MUST omit a section that would be empty, except the summary.

| # | Section | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `# ReShield Report` summary | Bullets for target, manifest, platform, assessment (complete or incomplete), overall level, score, score policy, severity policy, and not-checked levels when there are any. Followed by attained asset weight and critical assets below target when assets are declared |
| 2 | Scoring basis | One note stating what the overall level is based on, and that levels are unverified when the basis is declared only |
| 3 | `## Asset association and coverage limits` | Findings without an asset, findings that reference undeclared asset ids, and declared assets with no available level |
| 4 | `## Assets` | Table: asset, target level, observed level with confidence (or `undetermined` / `n/a`), meets target |
| 5 | `## Findings` | `fail` results only, most severe first. Each lists rule, location, current level with confidence, impact, severity basis, asset, evidence and fixes with target level and cost |
| 6 | `## Not assessed` | `open` and `error` results: rule, whether it could not complete or lacked information, location, reason, scope (asset or whole build) and evidence |
| 7 | `## Confirmed protection` | `pass` results: rule, level with confidence, location |
| 8 | Not applicable | One line listing `notapplicable` rule ids |

- `open` and `error` results MUST NOT appear under Findings, and `pass` results MUST NOT appear as problems.
- When the score is withheld or the overall level is an upper bound, the summary MUST say so and why, instead of showing a number.
- A report with no results MUST say that it establishes nothing about protection.

## Rule ids

`RS-<PLATFORM>-<NNN>`, for example `RS-UNITY-001`. Rules that apply to every platform use `RS-CORE-<NNN>`.

```

`protocol/README.md`:

# ReShield Protocol

**Status:** v0.1 (2026-11-01).

The ReShield Protocol standardizes how reverse-engineering protection is **declared**, **measured** and **reported**. It does not standardize a protection algorithm: protection stays diverse so that one generic tool cannot break every project.

| # | Spec | Status | v0.1 scope |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | [Asset declaration](01-asset-declaration.md) | v0.1 | Yes |
| 02 | [Protection levels](02-protection-levels.md) | v0.1 | Yes |
| 03 | [Transformation interface](03-transformation-interface.md) | Placeholder | No |
| 04 | [AI-resilience measurement](04-ai-resilience-measurement.md) | v0.1 method | Partial |
| 05 | [Report format](05-report-format.md) | v0.1 | Yes |
| — | [Adapter interface](adapter-interface.md) | Frozen v0.1 | Yes |

Machine-readable schemas live in [`schemas/`](schemas/).

Proposals: [packaged builds and layered scanning](proposals/v0.2-containers.md) (approved and implemented); [Unity evidence mapping and advanced analysis](proposals/v0.2-unity-analysis.md) (proposed for v0.2).

## Conventions

The key words MUST, SHOULD and MAY are used as described in RFC 2119.
