`protocol/adapter-interface.md`:

```md
# Adapter interface

**Status:** Frozen v0.1 (2026-10-31). Changes need maintainer approval and go through a versioned proposal, such as [`proposals/v0.2-unity-analysis.md`](proposals/v0.2-unity-analysis.md). `tests/test_freeze_contract.py` guards the frozen inputs and fields.

An adapter is the only platform-specific layer. It recognizes one kind of build and turns what it observes into findings. Scoring, reporting and remediation priorities live in the core.

Reference implementation: [`skills/reshield/scripts/reshield/adapters/base.py`](../skills/reshield/scripts/reshield/adapters/base.py)

## Contract

| Member | Description |
|---|---|
| `name` | Stable lowercase id, used in `--platform` and rule ids |
| `display_name` | Human-readable name |
| `checked_levels` | The levels whose criteria the adapter evaluates. The core reports the rest as not checked |
| `detect(target) -> bool` | Cheap check whether the target is a build for this platform |
| `scan(context) -> list[Finding]` | Inspect the build and return one result per evaluated rule |

## Result kinds

Every result carries a `kind`, taken from SARIF 2.1.0 `result.kind` (§3.27.9). `kind` defaults to `fail`, as in SARIF, so a result that only reports exposure needs no change.

| `kind` | Use when | `level` | `severity` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fail` | Exposure was found | The level observed | Any |
| `pass` | The protection conditions of a level were confirmed | The level supported | `info` |
| `open` | The rule was evaluated, but the build does not give enough information to decide (unsupported format version, conflicting or missing evidence) | `null` | `info` |
| `error` | Evaluation could not complete (permission denied, I/O failure) | `null` | `info` |
| `notapplicable` | The rule does not apply to this build | `null` | `info` |

`Finding` rejects any other combination when it is constructed.

A result with `asset_id` set applies to that asset. A result with `asset_id` empty applies to the whole build; an `open` or `error` result for the whole build leaves every asset undetermined.

## Rules

- Adapters MUST be read-only: never modify, execute or upload the target.
- Adapters MUST put what they observed in `Finding.evidence`.
- Adapters MUST return a result for every rule they evaluate. When a rule cannot be decided, return `open` or `error` with the reason in `impact`. An empty list, a log message or a warning MUST NOT stand for "could not tell".
- Adapters MUST set `Finding.confidence` honestly. A level that cannot be seen in the build is `inferred` or `declared`, never `observed`.
- Rule ids MUST follow `RS-<PLATFORM>-NNN`, where `<PLATFORM>` is the adapter `name` in upper case (for example `RS-UNITY-001`).
- Location paths MUST be relative to the scan target and use `/` as the separator. Absolute paths are rejected.
- Logic needed by two or more adapters SHOULD move to the core.

## Validation

`Finding` and `Location` validate themselves when constructed. An invalid rule id or an absolute path raises `ValueError`, so mistakes surface in tests instead of in reports.

## v0.1 integration review

Godot and Web/Electron were added after the freeze (2026-11-01) without any change to this interface. Core changes made at the same time were additive and live in the core, not in adapters:

| Change | Where | Why it stayed in the core |
|---|---|---|
| Severity normalization (severity-v1) | `severity.py`, `scanner.py` | Same consequence rules for every platform |
| Developer summary and `code_area()` | `actions.py`, `models.py` | Grouping by fix and plain wording apply to every platform |
| Plain-language fixes per rule | each adapter's `Remediation` | Fix steps are platform-specific, so they stay in adapters |

Differences that remain in adapters, by design: how each platform establishes confidence (Web reports readable JavaScript as `inferred`), and how results are grouped when no assets are declared. See `benchmarks/RESULTS.md`.

## Layered scanning (approved 2026-11-02)

The adapter contract is unchanged. The scanner decides which adapters run:

- **Folder or file builds:** the first adapter in registry order whose `detect()` matches, plus its companions (`unreal` and `android` also run `native`, because their code ships as native binaries).
- **Packaged builds** (`.apk`, `.aab`, `.ipa`, `.zip`, `.xapk`, `.apks`): every adapter that matches the extracted contents. Locations are prefixed with `<package>!/`.

## Adding a platform

1. Create `adapters/<name>.py` implementing `Adapter`.
2. Register it in `adapters/__init__.py`.
3. Add `references/remediation/<name>.md`.
4. Add tests and a benchmark sample.

```

`protocol/proposals/v0.2-containers.md`:

```md
# v0.2 proposal: packaged builds and layered scanning

Status: approved and implemented for v0.1 (2026-11-02).

## Problem

Many builds are archives or contain several technologies at once:

- Android `.apk` / `.aab`: Java or Kotlin `classes.dex`, native `.so` files, and possibly a Unity or Flutter build inside.
- iOS `.ipa`, Windows installers, `.zip` releases, Unreal `.pak` files.
- Electron apps with native add-ons; Unity builds with native plugins.

The frozen interface sends one build to one adapter, and the scanner cannot open archives. Until this proposal lands, the skill asks the developer to extract a copy and scans that.

## Proposed changes

1. **Container adapters.** An adapter that recognizes an archive opens it read-only, with size and entry-count budgets, and exposes its entries as a virtual file tree. Nothing is written to disk.
2. **Layered dispatch.** The scanner runs every adapter whose `detect()` matches a sub-tree, not only the first one, and merges their findings. Locations become `archive!/inner/path`.
3. **New adapters on top:** Android DEX (R8/ProGuard detection), Flutter AOT snapshots (`--obfuscate`), Unreal `.pak` (encryption flag).

## Interface impact

- `Adapter.detect()` and `scan()` stay the same; `ScanContext` gains an optional read-only file system object. Existing adapters keep working on a real directory.
- `Location.path` allows the `!/` separator for paths inside archives.

## As implemented

- Packages are extracted into a private temporary folder that is deleted after the scan, instead of a virtual file system, so the adapter interface did not change. The original file is only read. Limits: 200,000 entries, 8 GB extracted, a compression ratio of 200 per entry, and no absolute paths, `..` or links.
- Every adapter that recognizes the extracted tree runs. For folder builds, Unreal and Android also run the native adapter as a companion.
- New adapters: Android DEX (`RS-ANDROID-001`) and Unreal `.pak`/`.utoc` (`RS-UNREAL-001`). Unity recognizes Android and iOS layouts.
- Verified on real Unity 6000.3.8f1 Android builds (IL2CPP arm64 and Mono armv7), scanned directly as `.apk` files.

## Acceptance

- Real Android Unity, native and Kotlin builds scanned without extraction, with the same results as an extracted copy.
- No file written during a scan; budgets enforced against zip bombs.

```

`protocol/proposals/v0.2-unity-analysis.md`:

```md
# v0.2 Unity evidence mapping and advanced analysis proposal

Status: proposed for v0.2. Not part of v0.1.

## Background

v0.1 checks Unity builds for L0 and L1 with the standard library only, and keeps `ScanContext(target, manifest)`, the Finding format and the JSON, Markdown and SARIF outputs unchanged. Checking L2 (renamed or protected code) needs more: reading the build's assets and settings, an optional evidence file from the developer, and per-asset assessments. Those change public interfaces, so they are proposed here first.

## Interfaces requiring review

| Interface | Proposed change | Questions to resolve before approval |
|---|---|---|
| ScanContext | Optional evidence_path: Path | Third-party adapter compatibility, input errors, and supported scope |
| run_scan and CLI | evidence_path and --evidence | Platform restrictions, error exit codes, and existing command behavior |
| Finding | Optional assessment: AssetAssessment | Python construction, JSON Schema, SARIF, and downstream migration |
| Condition models | CheckStatus, ConditionCheck, AssetAssessment | Status definitions, immutability, types, and consistency validation |
| Asset summary | supported_level, supported_confidence, level_checks | Precedence relative to observed_level, completeness, and the lowest exposed level |
| Unity analysis interfaces | BuildReader, AssetLocator, CodeAnalyzer | Inject reading, location, and analysis through abstractions; keep scoring and reporting in the core |
| Report validator | Standalone read-only consistency validation | Existing and new contract versions, unknown results, invalid inputs, and output boundaries |

These are proposals; fields and types may change during review. Establish a versioning strategy, update the protocol and schemas, announce migration steps, and obtain maintainer approval before release.

## Dependency decisions

| Package | Proposed purpose | Original implementation version, unapproved |
|---|---|---|
| UnityPy | Parse release and debugging settings | 1.25.4 |
| capstone | Decode native instructions and extract features | 5.0.9 |
| pefile | Inspect PE exports and function ranges | 2024.8.26 |
| dnfile | Classify CLI signatures, MemberRef records, and CIL | 0.18.0 |

Keep the core dependency-free and place analysis in an explicitly selected extension with a separate dependency list. Review licenses, transitive dependencies, supported Python versions and operating systems, version maintenance, and resource limits for untrusted binary parsing. The versions above record the original implementation; they are not adoption recommendations.

Core imports, detection, and basic scanning must work without these packages. When a requested advanced check cannot run because a dependency is missing, report an unknown result and its limitation; do not automatically install packages, crash, or infer a higher level.

## Evidence and level invariants

- Declarations must not hide observations. Retain asset_id on matched exposure; omitted, conflicting, or stale mapping data must not clear it.
- Verify relative paths, SHA-256 hashes, actual names, and method tokens. Reject escapes, links, and duplicate identifiers. Mapping files cannot award a protection level.
- L2 requires complete verification of artifact readability, backend, asset coverage, name transformation, symbols, and release settings. Missing tools, unsupported formats, and unresolved conditions remain unknown. Cover confirmation, refutation, unknown results, and errors before adding L2 to checked_levels.
- L3/L4 candidate features do not establish verified transformation or a custom VM. Require reliable asset-to-function mapping and calibration applicable to the target artifact.
- L5 requires distinguishing core implementations from RPC/DTO code. Missing names do not prove implementation absence. Conflicts with observed name exposure must remain visible, not disappear through unlinking. Even when no core implementation is found, L5 remains declared.
- Known lower-level exposure takes precedence. Support fields must not bypass open/error results, the rule against scoring incomplete assessments, or target comparisons.
- Targets remain read-only: do not execute, load, or upload them. Native control samples do not establish calibration against a complete Unity release.

## Proposed delivery sequence and acceptance

1. Announce interface, report-field, versioning, and migration impacts; obtain maintainer approval.
2. Separately approve the analysis extension and dependency strategy; test missing dependencies and parser failures.
3. Deliver evidence verification and location independently, followed by fully tested L2 checks.
4. Establish real-artifact calibration and negative controls for L3/L4 before claiming those capabilities.
5. Validate the report tool and all output formats independently, ensuring new fields follow the public schemas.

Each step must keep frozen-release tests passing and run CI on Windows, macOS, Linux, and the declared Python versions. Record missing real artifacts and unverified platforms as limitations.

## Language

Code, comments, docstrings and documentation are in English. Translations go in separate `.zh-TW.md` files. No language exception applies.

```

`protocol/schemas/finding.schema.json`:

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
  "$id": "https://github.com/swimchicken777/reshield/protocol/schemas/finding.schema.json",
  "title": "ReShield finding",
  "description": "The result of evaluating one rule. kind follows SARIF 2.1.0 result.kind; see protocol/05-report-format.md.",
  "type": "object",
  "required": ["rule_id", "title", "platform", "kind", "severity", "level", "confidence", "location", "impact"],
  "properties": {
    "rule_id": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^RS-[A-Z]+-[0-9]{3}$" },
    "title": { "type": "string" },
    "platform": { "type": "string" },
    "kind": { "enum": ["fail", "pass", "open", "error", "notapplicable"] },
    "severity": { "enum": ["critical", "high", "medium", "low", "info"] },
    "level": { "oneOf": [{ "$ref": "#/$defs/level" }, { "type": "null" }] },
    "location": {
      "type": "object",
      "required": ["path"],
      "properties": {
        "path": { "type": "string" },
        "symbol": { "type": ["string", "null"] },
        "line": { "type": ["integer", "null"], "minimum": 1 }
      }
    },
    "impact": { "type": "string" },
    "confidence": { "enum": ["observed", "inferred", "declared"] },
    "evidence": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["description"],
        "properties": {
          "description": { "type": "string" },
          "artifact": { "type": ["string", "null"] }
        }
      }
    },
    "remediation": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["summary", "target_level", "performance_cost"],
        "properties": {
          "summary": { "type": "string" },
          "target_level": { "$ref": "#/$defs/level" },
          "performance_cost": { "type": "string" },
          "steps": { "type": "array", "items": { "type": "string" } }
        }
      }
    },
    "asset_id": { "type": ["string", "null"] }
  },
  "allOf": [
    {
      "if": { "properties": { "kind": { "enum": ["fail", "pass"] } } },
      "then": { "properties": { "level": { "$ref": "#/$defs/level" } } },
      "else": { "properties": { "level": { "type": "null" }, "severity": { "const": "info" } } }
    }
  ],
  "$defs": {
    "level": { "enum": ["L0", "L1", "L2", "L3", "L4", "L5"] }
  }
}

```

`skills/reshield/SKILL.md`:

```md
---
name: reshield
description: Check how well a game or app's code is protected before release, find leaked keys, and help fix it. Use when a developer wants to protect their own code or game logic from being copied or cloned, asks for a pre-release protection check of their project or build (Unity, Unreal, Godot, web, Electron, .NET, C/C++, Rust, Go, Swift, Android, iOS, Flutter, Python), or asks to apply that protection. Works on projects and release builds the user owns.
---

# ReShield

The people using this skill know their own code, not security. Tell them what is at risk and what to do about it, in plain words, and offer to do it for them.

## How to talk to the developer

Assume the person is a software engineer of any kind (games, web, mobile, desktop, backend) who **knows nothing about security or code protection**. They only want to know **where the problem is and how to fix it**. If they would have to look a word up, do not use it.

- **Keep it short.** The whole answer fits on one screen: a verdict, at most 3 things to fix, and an offer to fix them. Mention that there are more only if there are.
- **One plain sentence first**, about their product, not the technology: "Anyone can copy your pricing logic right now."
- **Each thing to fix has three lines:** *Where* (the file or folder), *Why* (what could happen, in one sentence) and *Fix* (the exact menu to click, setting to change or command to run).
- **No tables, no levels, no lists of what was not checked**, unless the developer asks.
- **Use the developer's language.**
- **Never say "safe".** If something could not be checked, say so in one sentence.
- **End with an offer:** "Want me to fix step 1 for you?"

Words to avoid, and what to say instead:

| Instead of | Say |
|---|---|
| technical terms for reading compiled code | open your release files with a free tool and read the code |
| obfuscate, obfuscation | scramble the names in your code |
| L0 | can be read almost exactly as you wrote it |
| L1 | people can still see the names in your code |
| L2 to L4 | harder to read |
| L5, server-side | runs on your server, never in the release |
| metadata, symbols, IL, binary, DEX, RTTI | the information that shows what your code is called |
| source map | a file that contains your original code |
| minify | squash the code |
| observed / inferred / declared | we checked your build / based on your settings / as you told us |
| rule ids, severity, score, policy names | leave out |

Setting names the developer has to click, such as "Scripting Backend: IL2CPP", are fine: they will see them on screen.

## Workflow

### 1. Find what to check

Use the paths the developer gave, otherwise the current project. Look for:

- **The source project:** identify it from its project file, for example Unity (`ProjectSettings/`), Unreal (`*.uproject`), Godot (`project.godot`), web or Electron (`package.json`), .NET (`*.csproj`), Rust (`Cargo.toml`), Go (`go.mod`), C/C++ (`CMakeLists.txt`, `*.vcxproj`), Xcode (`*.xcodeproj`), Android (`build.gradle`), Flutter (`pubspec.yaml`), Python (`pyproject.toml`). [references/project-checks.md](references/project-checks.md) lists what to read for each.
- **A release build, if there is one:** the folder or file players get. The scanner reads Unity (desktop, Android, iOS), Godot, Unreal, web and Electron builds, Android apps, any native program (`.exe`, `.dll`, `.so`, `.dylib`, Linux and macOS executables) and .NET assemblies, and scans `.apk`, `.aab`, `.ipa` and `.zip` packages directly. It also checks every build for leaked keys and shipped debug files.
- **Installers** (`.msi`, `.dmg`, setup programs): install or mount them, then scan the installed folder.

If the project is not clearly the developer's own, for example someone else's published game, ask before going further. Do not ask the developer to write any configuration.

### 2. Look at the project (no build needed)

Read [references/project-checks.md](references/project-checks.md). Read the engine settings and skim the scripts to find the valuable code (combat, economy, save data, licensing, networking) and obvious exposure such as a Mono backend or published source maps. Present these as predictions: "Based on your project settings, your build will likely...".

Also look for ways to get the valuable logic **without** reading the compiled code, such as debug commands, test entry points or command-line options that return results.

### 3. Confirm with the build

When a build exists, always run the summary first and base your answer on it:

```bash
python "<skill-dir>/scripts/reshield_cli.py" scan "<build>" --format summary
```

Replace `<skill-dir>` with this skill's folder. Use `--format json` only to look up a detail. If there is no build, offer to build once to confirm (say which tools that needs), or continue with the predictions. The build is read-only: never modify, run or upload it.

**Always compare the settings with the build.** Check whether the protection configured in the project (for example the scripting backend, script export mode or source map setting) is what the build actually ships. Look at build scripts and build profiles that can override settings. A mismatch is a **Must fix** item: "Your settings say X, but your build ships Y because Z." See "Settings versus build" in [references/project-checks.md](references/project-checks.md).

### 4. Learn what matters most

If the project has no `reshield.toml`, you **must** ask once, in the same answer as the first results: propose the likely valuable areas from step 2 or from "Your code we found" in the summary, in plain words ("your combat, shop and save code"), and ask which matter most. Then write `reshield.toml` in the project root (never inside the build) following [references/asset-declaration.md](references/asset-declaration.md), say in one line that you saved their choices, and scan again. If the developer would rather not answer, continue without it.

### 5. Report

Use exactly this shape, in the developer's language:

```
**<one plain sentence about what can be copied or stolen>**

Fix these first:
1. <what to fix>
   Where: <file or folder>
   Why: <what could happen, one sentence>
   Fix: <the exact menu path, setting or command>
2. ...   (at most 3 items)

Want me to fix step 1 for you?
```

If you could not check something, add one sentence: "We couldn't check X, so we can't say it's safe." If there is no `reshield.toml`, add one question: "Which part of your code matters most to you, for example payments, game rules or license checks?"

Before sending, check: it fits on one screen; none of the words in the table above appear; every item has a Where and a Fix with a real menu path, setting or command; it ends with the offer. Give the full technical report only when asked, from [references/technical-details.md](references/technical-details.md).

### 6. Apply fixes when asked

Follow "Applying fixes" in [references/project-checks.md](references/project-checks.md): say what will change and get a yes, change the source project only, rebuild and scan again to show the result, and treat server-side or architectural changes as a plan to agree on rather than something to rewrite alone.

## Rules

- Only check projects the developer owns or is authorized to test.
- Never modify, run or upload the build being assessed. Do not install tools without asking.
- Missing evidence is never protection. Keep "could not check" and "not checked yet" visible.
- A claim in `reshield.toml` never hides code that actually ships, for example logic declared as server-side.

```

  default_prompt: "Before I release my game, check how well its code is protected and what I should fix"

`skills/reshield/references/asset-declaration.md`:

```md
# Working with asset declarations

Use this reference when interpreting or proposing `reshield.toml`. This bundled execution guide follows `protocol/01-asset-declaration.md` in the source repository; that specification is the authoritative definition, not an installation prerequisite. Keep the field and default summary aligned with it when the protocol changes. This guide does not define new asset categories or scoring rules.

## Identify and confirm assets

Start with existing declarations and the developer's stated priorities. With source access, inspect relevant modules to propose concrete candidates such as proprietary algorithms, licensing logic or sensitive configuration. With build access only, explain that candidates may be incomplete. Do not infer business value from file size, obfuscation or ease of recovery.

For each candidate, ask what harm recovery would cause and what runtime or architectural costs are acceptable. Have the owner confirm the value using the protocol's existing categories (`critical`, `high`, `medium`, `low`), target level and performance budget. Do not introduce a separate value classification rubric or silently assign business priorities.

Keep unconfirmed candidates separate from declared assets. Do not write a manifest unless requested.

## Format and defaults

```toml
[project]
name = "Example Game"

[[asset]]
id = "combat-formulas"
description = "Damage calculations"
value = "critical"
paths = ["Assets/Scripts/Combat/**"]
symbols = ["MyGame.Combat.*"]
target_level = "L3"
performance_budget = "moderate"
server_side = false
```

- `id` is required, unique and kebab-case. Supply at least one of `paths` or `symbols`.
- `paths` are source globs relative to the project root: `*` stays within one folder, `**` spans folders. `symbols` match names in the build; `*` spans dots and slashes. Matching is case-sensitive; `?` matches one character (within one folder for paths).
- `value`: `critical`, `high`, `medium` (default), or `low`.
- Default `target_level`: critical → L3; high / medium → L2; low → L1. Explicit values may be L0–L5. These are goals, not observations.
- `performance_budget`: `none`, `low` (default), `moderate`, or `high`.
- `server_side` defaults to false. True defaults the target to L5. Server-side status is a developer declaration, not proof obtained from the absence of a finding.
- Asset matching uses the first declaration whose paths or symbols match. Put specific declarations before broad ones and review overlap.

Prefer stable source paths and known build symbols. Do not invent symbols when only source names are known. Do not put credential values in the declaration or report; identify their location with redacted evidence.

## Select the manifest

The scanner searches the target and its parents, stopping at the first repository root containing `.git`. Pass `--manifest "<path>"` when the intended file is elsewhere or selection would be ambiguous. Verify the JSON report's `manifest` field after scanning.

When creating or editing a requested declaration, keep it in the source project outside the assessed build. Review changes with the user. The scanner validates TOML, ids, enums and required selectors; if rejected, explain the error and correct it rather than silently dropping the declaration. Without a manifest, scanning can continue, but asset priorities and target compliance remain unconfirmed.

```

`skills/reshield/references/project-checks.md`:

```md
# Project checks and fixes

How to review a source project without a build, and how to apply fixes when the developer asks. Findings from a project are **predictions**: say "your build will likely..." and offer a build check to confirm.

## Unity

| What to read | What it tells you |
|---|---|
| `ProjectSettings/ProjectSettings.asset`, `scriptingBackend:` entry for the platform (for example `Standalone: 0`) | `0` is Mono: game code ships readable almost like source. `1` is IL2CPP: names and structure stay readable unless the metadata is protected |
| Build Profiles (Unity 6) | A profile can override the project setting; mention this when you cannot see the profile |
| `Packages/manifest.json`, `Assets/` | Whether an obfuscator or metadata-protection tool is installed |
| Scripts under `Assets/` | Which code holds valuable logic (combat, economy, save data, licensing, networking) and any hardcoded keys or server secrets |

Fixes you can apply when asked:

- **Switch to IL2CPP:** change the Scripting Backend in Player Settings for each target platform. Prefer the Unity Editor UI or an editor script (`PlayerSettings.SetScriptingBackend`) over hand-editing YAML. IL2CPP builds need the IL2CPP module and a C++ toolchain; check before promising a build.
- **Turn off Development Build** in the build settings or profile for release builds.
- **Hide names or protect metadata:** needs a third-party obfuscator. Recommend options; do not install tools without asking.

## Godot

| What to read | What it tells you |
|---|---|
| `export_presets.cfg`, `script_export_mode` | `0` ships GDScript as text, `1` as binary tokens, `2` as compressed tokens. Tokens still decompile to readable scripts |
| `export_presets.cfg`, `encrypt_pck`, `encrypt_directory` | Whether the pack is encrypted. Encryption needs export templates built with the key; the key ships inside the game, so it slows copying but does not stop it |
| `.cs` files and `*.csproj` | C# code ships as managed assemblies, readable almost like source |

Fixes you can apply when asked: set `script_export_mode` to `1` or `2`. Set up encryption only with the developer, because it needs custom export templates and key handling.

## Web and Electron

| What to read | What it tells you |
|---|---|
| Bundler config (`vite.config.*` `build.sourcemap`, `webpack.config.*` `devtool`, esbuild `sourcemap` and `minify`) | Whether production builds publish source maps (original code) and whether code is minified |
| `package.json` build scripts, Electron builder config | Whether app code ships as plain files or in `app.asar`. `asar` is an archive, not protection |
| Client code | Hardcoded API keys, pricing or unlock logic that should live on a server |

Fixes you can apply when asked: turn off production source maps, turn on minification.

## Other ecosystems

These are checked from the project; the scanner confirms native, .NET and every-build checks (keys, debug files) when a build exists.

| Ecosystem | What to read | What to look for | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unreal Engine | `*.uproject`, `Config/DefaultGame.ini`, `Config/DefaultCrypto.ini` | Shipping configuration for releases; pak encryption and signing (Project Settings, Crypto); Blueprint logic ships in `.pak` files that are readable without encryption | Package with the Shipping configuration, turn on pak encryption, keep debug files (`.pdb`) out of the package |
| .NET (WPF, WinForms, MAUI, MonoGame, console) | `*.csproj` | `PublishAot`, `PublishReadyToRun`, `DebugType`, `DebugSymbols` | Publish with Native AOT where possible (ReadyToRun still ships IL); do not ship `.pdb` files |
| C and C++ | `CMakeLists.txt`, `*.vcxproj`, Makefiles | Release builds with `-g` or `/DEBUG`, missing `strip` | Release flags without debug info, `strip` or `-s`, keep symbols privately |
| Rust | `Cargo.toml` `[profile.release]` | `debug = true`, missing `strip = true` | `strip = true`, `debug = false`, `panic = "abort"` to drop some paths |
| Go | build scripts, `.goreleaser.yaml` | Builds without `-ldflags="-s -w"` | Add `-s -w` and `-trimpath`; function names still remain in Go tables |
| Swift, Objective-C (Xcode) | `*.xcodeproj` build settings | `DEBUG_INFORMATION_FORMAT`, `STRIP_INSTALLED_PRODUCT`, `.dSYM` folders copied into the app | Strip release builds and keep `.dSYM` out of the shipped app |
| Android (Java, Kotlin) | `build.gradle` or `build.gradle.kts` | `minifyEnabled` / `isMinifyEnabled` false for release; keys in `strings.xml` or `BuildConfig` | Turn on R8 minification and obfuscation for release; move keys to a server |
| Flutter | `pubspec.yaml`, build scripts | Release builds without `--obfuscate --split-debug-info` | Build with `--obfuscate --split-debug-info=<folder>` and keep the folder privately |
| React Native | `metro.config.js`, `android/app/build.gradle` | Source maps bundled into the app, Hermes off | Turn on Hermes bytecode and keep source maps out of the app |
| Python (PyInstaller, Nuitka) | `*.spec`, build scripts | PyInstaller bundles `.pyc` files that decompile almost to source | Compile with Nuitka or Cython for valuable code, and keep secrets out of the bundle |

Every ecosystem: search the source for API keys, tokens, private keys and `.env` files that would ship, and for debug or test entry points that return valuable results.

## Settings versus build

Settings describe what the developer intended; the build is what players get. Compare them every time a build exists, and report a mismatch as a Must fix item with its cause.

| Engine | Setting | What the build shows |
|---|---|---|
| Unity | `scriptingBackend` in `ProjectSettings.asset` | Mono ships `<Name>_Data/Managed/Assembly-CSharp.dll` and a `MonoBleedingEdge` folder. IL2CPP ships `GameAssembly.dll` (or `.so`, `.dylib`) and `<Name>_Data/il2cpp_data/` |
| Godot | `script_export_mode` in `export_presets.cfg` | The scan reports whether scripts ship as text or tokens |
| Web | Source map and minify settings in the bundler config | `.map` files or `sourceMappingURL` comments in the output, and whether the code is minified |

Common causes of a mismatch: an editor build script that sets the backend itself (look for `PlayerSettings.SetScriptingBackend`, `BuildPipeline.BuildPlayer` or a `-Variant`/`-executeMethod` option in build scripts), a Unity 6 build profile that overrides Player Settings, a CI pipeline with its own settings, or a stale build made before the settings changed.

## Applying fixes

1. Say exactly what you will change and why, in one or two sentences. Wait for a yes.
2. Change the **source project** only. Never modify the build being assessed.
3. Prefer small, reversible changes. Point out that version control can undo them.
4. Rebuild if the developer agrees and the toolchain is available, then scan again and tell them what changed.
5. Some fixes are design decisions, such as moving pricing, rewards or anti-cheat to a server. Explain them and offer a plan; do not rewrite the architecture on your own.

```

`skills/reshield/references/protection-levels.md`:

```md
# Protection levels (summary for the skill)

This bundled summary supports result interpretation without the source repository. The authoritative definitions are maintained in `protocol/02-protection-levels.md` in that repository; keep this summary aligned when they change.

| Level | Meaning | Typical cost |
|---|---|---|
| L0 | Shipped as-is. Decompiles to something close to the original source. | none |
| L1 | Compiled, but symbols and metadata expose names and structure. | none |
| L2 | Symbols and metadata stripped or encrypted. Logic readable, intent is not. | negligible |
| L3 | Control flow and data transformed. Recovery needs dedicated deobfuscation. | moderate, on affected code |
| L4 | Critical functions virtualized. | high, on affected code |
| L5 | Asset never ships. Runs on a server. | network latency, server cost |

## How to read a level

- A level applies to one **asset**, not to the whole build.
- L0–L2 are checked directly in the build. L3–L4 are estimates. L5 comes from what the developer declared.
- Always tell the user which of these a level is, using the finding's `confidence` field.

Per-platform conditions are maintained in the repository specification and implemented by adapters. This summary is not a replacement for those checks. Use levels and confidence reported by the scanner; do not guess a higher level or require the repository specification merely to explain a reported result.

The frozen Unity adapter implements only L0/L1; L2-L4 remain not checked. `server_side=true` must not clear matched exposure evidence: observations take precedence over declarations, and L5/declared does not establish verified backend deployment.

```

`skills/reshield/references/remediation/README.md`:

```md
# Remediation catalog

Use this index after interpreting a result's kind, evidence, asset association and confidence. It routes existing rule IDs to bundled guides; it does not add detection rules, award protection levels, or automatically modify a project.

## Rule index

| Rule ID | Exposure | Guide | Availability |
|---|---|---|---|
| `RS-UNITY-001` | Managed game code ships with Mono | [Unity: RS-UNITY-001](unity.md#rs-unity-001) | Guide available; intended L1 target requires rebuilt-artifact verification |
| `RS-UNITY-002` | IL2CPP type and method names remain readable | [Unity: RS-UNITY-002](unity.md#rs-unity-002) | Guide available; intended L2 target is not verified by the current adapter |
| `RS-GODOT-001` | Original GDScript text in an unencrypted PCK | [Godot: RS-GODOT-001](godot.md#rs-godot-001) | Intended L1 target requires rebuilt text/token evidence |
| `RS-GODOT-002` | Binary tokens in an unencrypted PCK | [Godot: RS-GODOT-002](godot.md#rs-godot-002) | Intended L2 plan needs agreement; the current adapter cannot verify L2 |

| Platform | Guide | Current coverage |
|---|---|---|
| Every platform | [core.md](core.md) | RS-CORE-001 (keys and passwords), RS-CORE-002 (debug files) |
| Unity | [unity.md](unity.md) | The two indexed exposure rules |
| Godot | [godot.md](godot.md) | Text and uncompressed token exposure in standalone PCK formats 2/3; unsupported scope is listed in the guide |
| Web / Electron | [web.md](web.md) | RS-WEB-001, RS-WEB-002 |
| Native and .NET | [native.md](native.md) | RS-DOTNET-001, RS-NATIVE-001 |
| Android | [android.md](android.md) | RS-ANDROID-001 |
| Unreal Engine | [unreal.md](unreal.md) | RS-UNREAL-001 |

Do not invent future Godot or Web rule IDs to populate the index. Add entries when their rule contracts and corresponding guidance exist.

## Choose guidance by result

1. For a `fail` result, read its `remediation` suggestions and use the exact `rule_id` to locate the detailed guide. Verify that its preconditions match the evidence and affected asset before proposing an option. Unlinked findings still receive applicable guidance, but the owner must confirm the intended asset scope and value.
2. Compare options with the asset's target and performance budget. Runtime cost, engineering effort and compatibility are different constraints. Cost categories are estimates, not benchmarks; mark unknown costs and do not promise an acceptable budget without measurement.
3. For `open`, request or inspect the missing evidence identified in `impact`. For `error`, address the reported environment or I/O failure. The same rule ID can represent these outcomes: do not use its exposure guide to assert that exposure was confirmed or remediation succeeded.
4. `pass` and `notapplicable` do not require exposure remediation for that result. They do not establish that other assets or rules are protected.
5. Present recommendations without applying them during a read-only assessment. Building or executing a modified application belongs to a separately authorized developer workflow. A target level in a recommendation is an intention, not an observed result.

## Missing or conflicting guidance

- No index entry, missing section, or unmatched preconditions: state that catalog guidance is unavailable for this result. Do not select a similar rule just because its platform or title resembles it.
- A Finding can still contain useful `remediation` when no detailed guide exists. Present it as an adapter suggestion and disclose the missing catalog entry.
- A guide can provide context when the Finding lacks suggestions, but keep catalog guidance separate from scanner output. Do not fabricate evidence or change the original Finding or its exported JSON/SARIF.
- Additional AI suggestions must be identified as supplemental, with their basis and uncertainty; do not describe them as approved catalog content.
- If a guide and adapter suggestion disagree on applicability, target, steps or cost, disclose the discrepancy and investigate the matching rule version before recommending the disputed action. Do not silently override either source.
- An option that exceeds the budget is not automatically forbidden, but must be labeled as requiring the owner's trade-off decision. Do not silently lower the target to make it fit.

## Verification and report boundary

Re-scan the intended rebuilt artifact with the same asset scope and declaration, with reports outside the build. Compare actual evidence, asset linkage, confidence, and supported checks. A disappearing Finding, an unsupported format, `open/error`, or a higher score does not independently establish the intended protection level.

JSON exports include existing `Finding.remediation` data, and Markdown reports list the adapter's concise fix suggestions. The current SARIF export does not include remediation, fixes or rule help. None of these formats automatically loads this Markdown catalog or embeds its extended costs and verification notes. Use the readable AI assessment or an accompanying note for that context. No new output fields, report loader, automatic fix, or SARIF patch is introduced here.

## Maintainer contract

- Add an exact rule-ID index entry and a stable anchor in the platform guide together. Keep the guide within the installed skill; required instructions cannot depend on repository-only files or network access.
- Adapter remediation is the concise scanner suggestion; the guide is its expanded context. Changes to target, applicability, core steps or cost need both reviewed together. This is intentional manual synchronization until a separately designed single-source system is adopted.
- Keep each rule's applicability and evidence distinct from generic platform background. Use current supported checks when describing verification, and explicitly identify checks that remain manual or unavailable.
- Add source references for technical recommendations. Unknown measurements stay unknown. Do not copy benchmark expectations into observed scan conclusions.
- When reviewing additions, check that the index link resolves, the rule exists, the intended target agrees with the adapter, and non-fail outcomes are not described as exposure. Check installation-relative links after copying the skill directory.

## Entry template

Use one anchored section per registered rule. Multiple options can have different costs and intended targets.

```markdown
<a id="rs-platform-nnn"></a>
## RS-PLATFORM-NNN: Rule title

**Intended target:** L<n>; subject to evidence-based verification.
**Applies when:** Required result kind, evidence and relevant asset scope.

### Options and steps

Describe the available choices, their preconditions, and concrete developer steps.

### Costs and compatibility

- Runtime cost: estimate or unknown; specify what must be measured.
- Engineering effort: implementation, rebuild and maintenance work.
- Compatibility: behavior and integration risks that require regression checks.

### Verify and remaining limits

Describe required rebuilt-artifact observations, expected result changes,
unsupported checks and how to interpret unknown or failed verification.

### Sources

Link technical sources. Do not use sources as evidence about this build.
```

```

`skills/reshield/references/remediation/android.md`:

```md
# Android remediation

<a id="rs-android-001"></a>
## RS-ANDROID-001: Java and Kotlin class names

**Intended target:** L2. **Runtime cost:** none; apps usually get smaller.

**Applies when:** your own classes in `classes*.dex` keep readable names, so free tools such as jadx rebuild near-source Java with your original names. Library classes (AndroidX, Kotlin, Google, Unity, Flutter) and generated classes (`R`, `BuildConfig`) are ignored.

### Steps

1. In the app module's `build.gradle`, set `minifyEnabled true` for the release build type (`isMinifyEnabled = true` in Kotlin scripts).
2. Add keep rules in `proguard-rules.pro` only for names that reflection, JSON parsing or native code need.
3. Build the release, test it, and scan the new `.apk` or `.aab`.

### Verify

The result becomes `pass` at L2 when most class names are short renamed letters. Logic can still be decompiled; move valuable rules and keys to a server.

```

`skills/reshield/references/remediation/core.md`:

```md
# Cross-platform remediation

Checks that run on every build, whatever the engine or language.

<a id="rs-core-001"></a>
## RS-CORE-001: A key or password ships with the build

**Severity:** critical. **Intended target:** L5 (the key no longer ships).

**Applies when:** the build contains an API key, access token or private key (in text or in a compiled file, including UTF-16 strings), or a credential file such as `.keystore`, `.jks`, `.p12`, `.pfx` or `.env`. Only high-precision patterns are reported, and the key is never shown in full.

### Steps

1. Treat the key as leaked: revoke or rotate it now. Removing it from the next build does not undo the leak.
2. Move calls that need the key to your own server, and let the game or app call that server.
3. Build again and scan; the result should be gone.

<a id="rs-core-002"></a>
## RS-CORE-002: Debug files ship with the build

**Intended target:** L2 for those names. **Runtime cost:** none.

**Applies when:** the build contains symbol files (`.pdb`, `.mdb`, `.debug`, `.sym`, `.dSYM`) or Unity folders that are not meant to ship (`*_BackUpThisFolder_ButDontShipItWithYourGame`, `*_BurstDebugInformation_DoNotShip`). The IL2CPP backup folder contains generated C++ source of your game and is reported at L0.

### Steps

1. Keep debug files for your own crash reports, but exclude them from the release package.
2. For Unity, delete the `DontShipItWithYourGame` and `DoNotShip` folders before uploading.
3. Package again and scan; the result should be gone.

<a id="rs-core-004"></a>
## RS-CORE-004: Code in a runtime ReShield cannot check yet

**Result:** `open`. Missing evidence is never counted as protection.

**Applies when:** the build contains Python (PyInstaller archives, `.pyc`), Flutter (`libapp.so`), React Native (`index.android.bundle`, `main.jsbundle`), Java (`.jar`, `.class`), Lua, GameMaker (`data.win`), Ren'Py (`.rpyc`, `.rpa`) or Xamarin / .NET MAUI (`assemblies.blob`) code. Without this rule, only the runtime's executable would be checked and the app could look protected.

### Steps

| Runtime | First step |
|---|---|
| Python | Compile the code you care about with Nuitka or Cython instead of shipping `.pyc` files |
| Flutter | `flutter build <platform> --obfuscate --split-debug-info=<private folder>` |
| React Native | Turn on Hermes and do not ship source maps |
| Java | Run ProGuard or another Java obfuscator on the release `.jar` |
| Lua | Do not ship plain `.lua` files; compile and encrypt them with your engine's tools |
| GameMaker | Build with the YYC (compiled) target instead of VM |
| Ren'Py | Keep important rules and keys on a server; scripts cannot be fully hidden |
| Xamarin / .NET MAUI | Turn on AOT and trimming, and use a .NET obfuscator |

For every runtime, keep keys and important rules on your server.

<a id="rs-core-005"></a>
## RS-CORE-005: A protection tool was applied

**Result:** `pass` at L2, `inferred`.

**Applies when:** the build shows the marks of VMProtect, Themida, WinLicense or Enigma Protector (section names in a Windows executable header), ConfuserEx, Dotfuscator, SmartAssembly or Babel (attributes in a .NET assembly), Obfuscator-LLVM (compiler version string) or javascript-obfuscator (at least 200 `_0x` names in one file).

Most of these tools protect only the code you select. Check that your important code is included, and keep the tool turned on for every release.

## Limits

The checks read up to 4 GB and 50,000 files per build; beyond that they report `open`. Generic passwords and custom token formats are not detected. Protection tools that leave no mark, such as Obfuscar or Eazfuscator, are not recognized.

```

`skills/reshield/references/remediation/godot.md`:

```md
# Godot remediation

This guide covers GDScript exposure checks in standalone PCK formats 2/3. Scanning is read-only; fixes require separate agreement, source-project changes and verification against a rebuilt artifact.

The implementation has been validated with synthetic samples; real Godot export acceptance was not performed in this change. Asset matching uses actual pack resource identities rather than guessed source directories. Directory scans cover immediate PCKs and code candidates, not the entire release tree recursively.

<a id="rs-godot-001"></a>
## RS-GODOT-001: Original script text exposed

**Intended target:** L1, subject to rebuilt-artifact verification.

**Applies when:** The result is `fail` and evidence verifies an unencrypted PCK entry's hash and readable GDScript structure. C# candidates or unreadable scripts produce `open` and do not establish this exposure.

### Options and steps

1. In the source project's target export preset, set `script_export_mode` to `1` to export uncompressed binary tokens.
2. Export to a clean output directory so stale `.gd` text does not ship alongside tokens.
3. Run behavior and loading-performance checks, then scan the new artifact read-only.

This change protects against direct reading of original text. Tokens still retain analyzable names and logic; they do not prevent reconstruction.

### Costs and compatibility

- Runtime cost: estimated low, not measured; compare startup, resource loading and memory usage.
- Engineering effort: adjust the export preset, rebuild and run behavior regressions.
- Compatibility: verify that plugins, dynamic loading and export filters do not depend on original script text.
- Mode `2` uses compressed tokens. The scanner does not decompress them and reports uncertainty; that result cannot verify an improvement.

### Verify and remaining limits

Confirm that `RS-GODOT-001` text exposure is replaced by valid `RS-GODOT-002` token evidence. Keep the same asset declaration and verify `.gd.remap` preserves the original resource identity. A disappearing Finding alone does not establish target attainment.

<a id="rs-godot-002"></a>
## RS-GODOT-002: Binary tokens remain analyzable

**Intended target:** L2, through a protection plan requiring agreement first; the current adapter cannot verify L2.

**Applies when:** The result is `fail` and an unencrypted entry's hash and uncompressed tokenizer v101 structure are verified. The scanner checks identifiers, value constants, line/column mappings and token bounds without executing or decompiling scripts. Other versions and unsupported Variant constants remain unknown.

Supported value constants are nil, bool, int, float, String and StringName. Annotation and ordinary identifiers are validated against their token kinds. Remaps accept only an explicit lowercase `path` string in a single `[remap]` section. DEFAULT inheritance, other structures, duplicates and ambiguous mappings remain unknown and cannot establish asset linkage.

### Options and steps

1. Agree on encryption scope, key handling and custom export-template maintenance with the developer.
2. Configure PCK/directory encryption using custom templates built with the key; check for remaining plaintext copies.
3. If C# assemblies exist, assess name obfuscation and release layout separately; PCK encryption does not automatically protect external assemblies.
4. Verify behavior, performance and exposure against a new build. Encryption flags, unreadability and `open` results do not independently prove L2.

### Costs and compatibility

- Runtime cost: unknown; measure startup and resource loading rather than promising compliance with a budget.
- Engineering effort: custom template builds, key injection, CI and per-platform version maintenance.
- Compatibility: templates must match the key and engine version; regress dynamic resources, plugins and updates.
- The key ships in the game template. Encryption raises analysis cost but does not guarantee prevention of reconstruction.

### Verify and remaining limits

Encryption scope, plaintext copies and C# protection currently require independent manual checks or separately approved tools. Encrypted data remains `open`; the scanner does not award an L2 `pass`. Embedded PCKs, Godot 3, compressed tokens, C#, GDExtension and L2-L4 are unverified. L5 remains declared.

### Sources

- [Godot 4.5.1 export preset definitions](https://github.com/godotengine/godot/blob/4.5.1-stable/editor/export/editor_export_preset.h)
- [Godot 4.5.1 PCK reader and encryption flags](https://github.com/godotengine/godot/blob/4.5.1-stable/core/io/file_access_pack.cpp)
- [Godot 4.5.1 tokenizer format](https://github.com/godotengine/godot/blob/4.5.1-stable/modules/gdscript/gdscript_tokenizer_buffer.cpp)

These references explain formats; they do not establish that a particular assessed artifact satisfies the conditions.

```

`skills/reshield/references/remediation/native.md`:

```md
# Native and .NET remediation

Fixes for programs scanned by the native fallback adapter.

<a id="rs-dotnet-001"></a>
## RS-DOTNET-001: .NET code ships as readable IL

**Intended target:** L1. **Runtime cost:** low; startup is usually faster with Native AOT.

**Applies when:** one of your own .NET assemblies (not the framework) ships with readable IL. Free decompilers turn it back into near-source C#.

### Steps

1. Publish with Native AOT (.NET 7 or later), which ships machine code instead of IL. ReadyToRun alone is not enough: it still includes the IL.
2. If Native AOT does not fit (reflection-heavy code, plugins), run an obfuscator on your own assemblies before packaging.
3. Build again and scan; your assemblies should no longer appear as readable code.

<a id="rs-native-001"></a>
## RS-NATIVE-001: Program names and structure

**Intended target:** L2. **Runtime cost:** none.

**Applies when:** a native binary ships names an attacker can use: a symbol table, DWARF debug information, C++ RTTI class names, Go function tables, or Objective-C/Swift class names. A `pass` at L2 means none were found; it is an estimate, because strings and machine code stay readable.

### Steps

1. Build in release mode and strip symbols: MSVC without `/DEBUG` in the shipped binary, GCC/Clang with `-s` or `strip`, Rust with `strip = true` in the release profile, Go with `-ldflags="-s -w"`.
2. Keep the stripped symbols privately for crash reports.
3. Names that must stay (RTTI, Go function tables, Swift and Objective-C classes) need an obfuscator or code changes to hide.

### Verify

Scan the new build. A stripped binary returns `pass` at L2 (estimated) instead of `fail` at L1.

```

`skills/reshield/references/remediation/unity.md`:

```md
# Unity findings and remediation

This adapter inspects Windows and Linux Player root directories, macOS `.app` bundles, Android and iOS packages, and WebGL build folders. It only reads build artifacts; installing Unity or running the game is not required. A finding represents evidence of exposed code, names, or structure. The scripting backend itself is not a vulnerability.

<a id="rs-unity-001"></a>
## RS-UNITY-001: Managed game code ships with the Mono build

**Intended target:** L1 (must still be verified against the rebuilt artifacts)

**Applies when:** A `fail` result confirms the following evidence: The same Player contains a valid Mono runtime and game assemblies with parseable Common Language Infrastructure (CLI) metadata and IL method bodies.

### Options and steps

The primary option is to switch the affected Player build to IL2CPP. If the platform or project dependencies prevent that change, retain the current backend only as an explicit trade-off: the observed L0 exposure has not been remediated, and another protection approach requires separate evaluation.

1. Set Scripting Backend to IL2CPP in Player Settings for the target platform.
2. Clean the previous output and rebuild to avoid mixing Mono and IL2CPP artifacts.
3. Validate reflection, generics, serialization, native plugins, and performance. Adjust preservation rules as needed.
4. Inspect the metadata and symbols included in the build. Switching backends alone does not guarantee L2.

### Costs and compatibility

- **Runtime cost:** the adapter's concise estimate is low. Actual CPU time, startup latency and memory impact are workload-dependent and must be measured; a low category does not prove the asset's performance budget is met.
- **Engineering effort:** unknown until a trial build. Plan backend/toolchain setup, clean rebuilds, regression checks and ongoing maintenance. Build-time cost is separate from runtime cost.
- **Compatibility:** verify reflection, generic instantiation, serialization, native plugins and target-platform support. Keep required preservation rules and test game behavior; switching backends is not a drop-in guarantee.

### Verify and remaining limits

Run the existing `scan` command against the new Player root directory. If IL2CPP and readable metadata names are confirmed, the finding should change to `RS-UNITY-002` at L1. An `open` or `error` result means the tool could not decide or could not complete the assessment; it does not establish that remediation succeeded.

### Sources

See the [Unity scripting backend overview](https://docs.unity3d.com/jp/current/Manual/scripting-backends-intro.html) and [Windows IL2CPP artifact reference](https://docs.unity3d.com/cn/2023.1/Manual/WindowsPlayerIL2CPPScriptingBackend.html). They describe the backend and artifact model, not a measured performance guarantee for this project.

<a id="rs-unity-002"></a>
## RS-UNITY-002: IL2CPP metadata exposes game type and method names

**Intended target:** L2 (all protocol criteria must be met)

**Applies when:** A `fail` result for `RS-UNITY-002` confirms a structurally valid native library and readable game-name exposure in the same Player. For non-v31 metadata, the adapter can establish this from the standard header and readable game assembly/namespace names without parsing the layout. v31 can additionally provide validated sections and individual method references; those details are not prerequisites for using this guide with other supported name-exposure results.

### Options and steps

Choose targeted name obfuscation or metadata protection based on the asset's exposure and compatibility needs. They are alternatives, not a requirement to deploy every technique. No protection product is endorsed; feasibility and actual cost require a trial on this project's build pipeline.

1. Choose name obfuscation or metadata protection appropriate to the sensitive assets. No specific product is required.
2. Preserve names required by reflection, serialization, and external interfaces. Validate functionality, startup time, memory usage, and performance.
3. Check whether the final artifacts still contain debug symbols or development-build flags. Their absence is also required for L2.
4. Verify the protection against the actual artifacts, rather than relying solely on tool settings or changes to the metadata header.

### Costs and compatibility

- **Runtime cost:** the adapter's concise estimate is moderate. Actual costs depend on the protection method and affected code. Measure startup, CPU time and memory; do not infer a fixed slowdown from this category.
- **Engineering effort:** unknown until tools and scope are selected. Account for build integration, platform/version support, debugging, reproducibility, validation and maintenance.
- **Compatibility:** preserve externally referenced names and test reflection, serialization, native interfaces and error reporting. Review development flags and shipped symbols independently of metadata protection.

### Verify and remaining limits

Scan again and check which sensitive names remain readable. A changed format, missing file, or unsupported format produces an `open` result for `RS-UNITY-002`, not an L2 rating. Encryption or obfuscation and the other L2 criteria require separate verification. The current adapter checks L0/L1 only and cannot award L2 or prove this fix complete. If the required verification is unavailable, report the intended target as unverified; do not increase the observed level or score manually.

### Sources

Consult the [Unity scripting backend overview](https://docs.unity3d.com/jp/current/Manual/scripting-backends-intro.html) and [Windows IL2CPP artifacts](https://docs.unity3d.com/cn/2023.1/Manual/WindowsPlayerIL2CPPScriptingBackend.html). These describe platform behavior, not measured performance or evidence of this build's protection. Cost estimates above follow the existing adapter suggestions and require project-specific measurement.

## Detection coverage and limitations

- Desktop layouts are inspected at fixed locations: `_Data` and `.app/Contents/Resources/Data`, identified by `globalgamemanagers` or `data.unity3d`. The latter is not decompressed, and project or backup directories are not searched recursively.
- Supported Mono runtime locations are `MonoBleedingEdge/EmbedRuntime/mono-2.0-bdwgc.dll` on Windows, `<Product>_Data/MonoBleedingEdge/x86_64/libmonobdwgc-2.0.so` on Linux, and `Contents/Frameworks/MonoBleedingEdge/MonoEmbedRuntime/osx/libmonobdwgc-2.0.dylib` on macOS. Other layouts remain unknown.
- Supported IL2CPP native libraries are `GameAssembly.dll` on Windows, `GameAssembly.so` on Linux, and `Contents/Frameworks/GameAssembly.dylib` on macOS. Metadata is expected at `il2cpp_data/Metadata/global-metadata.dat` within the data directory.
- WebGL is always IL2CPP. The metadata is read from `Il2CppData/Metadata/global-metadata.dat` inside `Build/<name>.data` (plain or Gzip). Brotli-compressed data cannot be read with the Python standard library and is reported `open`; set Compression Format to Gzip or Disabled to check it.
- CLI metadata parsing currently supports `#~` and `#Strings` and checks that methods have IL bodies. `#-` pointer tables are not supported. Known Unity and .NET runtime assembly names are excluded, but custom plugins may still be reported as shipped application code. Whether they belong to sensitive assets depends on evidence-based manifest matching.
- IL2CPP metadata is checked in two ways. **Every version:** the file must start with the standard IL2CPP header, and the adapter reads it the way an analyst would first look, by checking whether game assembly names and game namespaces (or the manifest's declared symbols) are stored as plain text. If they are, the names are exposed (L1, observed), whatever the version. **v31 only:** the layout was checked against `GlobalMetadataFileInternals.h` shipped with Unity 2022.3.62f2 and 6000.0.68f1, so the adapter also lists individual methods and validates every section. The metadata version is recorded in the evidence but is not needed for the verdict.
- A nonstandard header (possibly encrypted or customized), names that cannot be confirmed, a damaged v31 layout, oversized data, conflicting evidence, or multiple Players produce an `open` result, never a guessed protection level. A game that uses only the global namespace needs `symbols` in `reshield.toml` for names to be confirmed in versions other than v31.
- Reads are bounded. Symbolic links, Windows junctions, and case-colliding names encountered during inspection are rejected. If an assembly cannot be parsed, the adapter takes a conservative approach: it returns an `open` result and no `fail` findings from a partially successful scan.
- Asset matching uses actual method names and paths relative to the build root. Source files and nested-type relationships are not fabricated from method names. Unmatched findings retain `asset_id=None`; the same evidence is not copied to every declared asset.
- `server_side=true` is not an exclusion condition. Retain matched exposure associations and include them in the summary; an L5 declaration cannot override observations from shipped artifacts.
- Undecided rules are reported as `open` (not enough information) or `error` (could not complete), with the reason in `impact`. The scan is then marked incomplete and no score is given. When the backend is identified, the other backend's rule is reported as `notapplicable`. When a manifest is present, unmatched findings may not contribute to the asset score.
- The adapter evaluates L0 and L1 criteria only. Reports list L2–L4 as not checked.
- Temporary synthetic format samples exercise parser branches; they do not establish that every Unity version or real build artifact has been validated.

References: [Unity scripting backends](https://docs.unity3d.com/jp/current/Manual/scripting-backends-intro.html), [Unity Windows IL2CPP build artifacts](https://docs.unity3d.com/cn/2023.1/Manual/WindowsPlayerIL2CPPScriptingBackend.html), [Cpp2IL desktop artifact discovery](https://github.com/SamboyCoding/Cpp2IL/blob/development/Cpp2IL/Program.cs).

```

`skills/reshield/references/remediation/unreal.md`:

# Unreal Engine remediation

<a id="rs-unreal-001"></a>
## RS-UNREAL-001: Packaged Unreal content

**Intended target:** L2. **Runtime cost:** a small load-time cost.

**Applies when:** a `.pak` or IoStore `.utoc` container's index is not encrypted, so free tools list and extract every asset and Blueprint.

### Steps

1. In Project Settings, Crypto, generate an encryption key, turn on **Encrypt Pak Index** and **Encrypt Assets**, and turn on **Sign Pak Files**.
2. Package with the **Shipping** configuration and keep `.pdb` files out of the package.
3. Scan the packaged folder again.

### Verify

Each container returns `pass` at L2 (estimated). The key ships inside the game executable, so encryption slows extraction but does not stop a determined attacker. Game C++ code is checked separately as `RS-NATIVE-001`.
