`skills/reshield/references/remediation/web.md`:

```md
# Web / Electron remediation

Fixes for published JavaScript, source maps and Electron `app.asar` content. Scanning is read-only; fixes change the source project and need a rebuild and a new scan to verify.

<a id="rs-web-002"></a>
## RS-WEB-002: Published source map

**Intended target:** L1. **Runtime cost:** none. **Effort:** minutes.

**Applies when:** the result is `fail` and a published `.map` file (or inline map) embeds the original source in `sourcesContent`. This is the most serious Web exposure: anyone can read the original code with names and comments.

### Steps

1. Turn off source maps for production builds: Vite `build.sourcemap = false`, webpack `devtool = false`, esbuild without `--sourcemap`.
2. If you need source maps for error reports, upload them privately to your error tracker instead of shipping them.
3. Build to a clean output folder.

### Verify

Scan the new build. No `RS-WEB-002` result should remain, and no `.map` files or `sourceMappingURL` comments should be in the output.

<a id="rs-web-001"></a>
## RS-WEB-001: Published application code

**Applies when:** the result is `fail` for shipped JavaScript. The level is an estimate (`inferred`) based on whether functions keep descriptive parameter names.

### At L0: readable code

**Intended target:** L1. **Runtime cost:** none.

1. Turn on minification in the bundler for production builds.
2. Build to a clean output folder and check the app still works.

Minified code can be reformatted and its property names and strings stay readable, so this only raises the effort.

### At L1: minified code

**Intended target:** L5 for logic that matters. **Cost:** network latency, server cost and engineering effort.

Anything that runs in the browser or in Electron can be read. Keep pricing, rewards, unlock checks and API keys on a server, and let the client only send requests. Property mangling or a commercial obfuscator raises the cost further, but ReShield cannot verify those protections yet.

### Verify

Scan the new build and compare the level and the files listed. An `open` result means the code could not be analyzed, not that it is protected.

## Not covered yet

Electron `app.asar` is an archive, not protection: code inside it is reported the same way as loose files. WebAssembly, property mangling and string encryption are not checked in this release.

```

`skills/reshield/references/report-format.md`:

```md
# Report format

Read this reference before delivering a **full technical report**. For developers, lead with the developer summary (`scan --format summary`, or `actions` in JSON) as described in `SKILL.md`; use this structure when they ask for the complete report, CI output or every finding. It is bundled so a copied skill does not need the source repository. The authoritative maintenance specification is `protocol/05-report-format.md` in the ReShield repository; keep this reference aligned with it.

## Shared readable report structure

Use the following order for every platform and for reports written from CLI JSON. Preserve the canonical section labels below. Omit sections with no content, except the summary. A short chat handoff may link the full report; it does not replace a requested complete report.

| Order | Section | Required content |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `# ReShield Report` summary | Target, manifest (or none), platform, assessment completeness, overall level, score, `asset-target-v1` score policy, severity policy when recorded, and unchecked levels when present. When assets are declared, follow with attained asset weight and critical assets below target when any exist. |
| 2 | Scoring basis note | State whether the overall level uses declared assets with available levels or the fail/pass fallback without declared assets. If levels are declared only, identify them as unverified. |
| 3 | `## Asset association and coverage limits` | Results without an asset association in a declared scope, references to undeclared asset ids, and declared assets without available levels. Include affected rule ids and asset ids. Distinguish `no_results` from `undetermined`. |
| 4 | `## Assets` | Table: asset, target level, observed level with confidence (or `undetermined` / `n/a`), meets target. Preserve unknown comparisons; label inferred/declared comparisons as unverified. |
| 5 | `## Findings` | Only `fail`, most severe first. Each entry gives rule id, title, severity, location, current level with confidence, impact, Severity basis when available, asset association, evidence, and fixes with target level and cost. Mark missing evidence or fixes as not provided. |
| 6 | `## Not assessed` | Only `open` and `error`: rule, title, insufficient information versus inability to complete, location, reason, scope (asset or whole build), and available evidence. |
| 7 | `## Confirmed protection` | Only `pass`: rule, title, level with confidence, location. A pass does not override exposure for the same asset. |
| 8 | Not-applicable line | List all `notapplicable` rule ids in one final line, for example `Rules not applicable to this build: RS-UNITY-001`. |

Preserve available line numbers and symbols in locations. Missing location detail is not permission to infer it. `open` and `error` are diagnostics, not confirmed exposures; `pass` is not a problem. Keep each in its own section. Not-applicable results establish no protection level.

Unassociated whole-build `open/error` results still affect completeness and make every declared asset undetermined. An undeclared asset reference remains a coverage problem; do not silently attach it to a similarly named asset. Without declared assets, unassociated results are the normal fallback scope, not a manifest-selector error. Explicit references to nonexistent asset ids must still be disclosed.

## Completeness, confidence and score

- Use the existing `summary.complete`, `summary.assets`, `summary.not_checked_levels` and score; do not recalculate conclusions from missing evidence. Complete means no `open/error`, not that every protection level or asset was verified.
- When incomplete, show the overall level only as an upper bound if a level exists, with the reason. Keep the score withheld. When no level exists, report `n/a` rather than inventing an upper bound.
- A numeric asset-target-v1 score requires declared assets, complete assessment, and an available `observed`-confidence level for every declared asset. Unknown, inferred-only or declared-only asset levels withhold it even when complete is true. Explain why in the summary.
- Weights are critical=4, high=3, medium=2, low=1, each asset counted once. When scoring is eligible, show attained weight / total declared weight alongside the existing score. When withheld, show attained weight as withheld with its reason and retain the total declared weight; do not display an unverified numerator or discard unknown assets from the denominator.
- Keep `observed`, `inferred` and `declared` next to their levels. A server-side L5 declaration is unverified; not finding code is not independent evidence of deployment.
- The percentage measures declared target attainment, not absolute protection or measured recovery difficulty. Observed L0 at target L0 may score 100. Retain overall levels, confidence, critical asset gaps and coverage limits. Compare only unchanged scope, values, targets and policy. Dev0 placeholder scores are not comparable.
- A report with no results must explicitly state that the scan establishes nothing about protection.

## Supplemental notes and structured exports

After the canonical body and final not-applicable line, place source availability, execution environment, platform-selection evidence, additional AI observations and prioritized next actions in a clearly labeled supplemental note. Preserve scanner findings separately from AI guidance, and identify missing estimates or contradictory inputs. Do not change findings, asset associations, levels or scores to make the report appear complete.

JSON and SARIF export scanner data, not supplemental AI review. For multiple exports, use the same target, manifest and platform and disclose material differences if checks rerun. JSON retains its existing fields, including `null` scores; no score-policy or score-reason field is added. SARIF retains its existing kind mapping, non-fail `level: "none"`, error execution notifications, and tool version, without a numeric score field.

Before claiming delivery, confirm that requested report files exist, are readable and are outside the assessed build. State the artifact paths and whether the assessment is partial.

## Severity policy and rationale

Core scans use `severity-v1`; see `severity-policy.md`. Preserve exported severities rather than applying the matrix again. CLI Markdown records the policy and detailed Severity basis. JSON does not include the internal rationale: when reconstructing a report from JSON, use the corresponding Markdown if available; otherwise state that detailed severity basis was not provided. Never present an inferred explanation as scanner-recorded rationale. Older or manually supplied results without recorded normalization must not be labeled policy-normalized.

After changing findings or declarations through library code, rerun normalization and summary calculation before exporting. The Markdown rationale guard detects changed decision inputs; it does not refresh severities, summary statistics or scores.

```

`skills/reshield/references/severity-policy.md`:

```md
# Reading contextual severity

Scans through the core use `severity-v1`. Severity expresses the potential consequence of a known implementation exposure for the associated asset, not a CVSS rating, active incident, or proof that copying occurred. It is separate from protection level, confidence and `asset-target-v1` target attainment.

## Registered rule meanings

| Rule | Exposure class | Unassociated baseline |
|---|---|---|
| RS-UNITY-001 | source-like-implementation | high |
| RS-UNITY-002 | readable-names-and-structure | medium |

Non-v31 IL2CPP results do not need parsed type/method records to use the second class: confirmed readable game-name exposure is enough under that rule's existing contract. This policy does not broaden detection.

| Exposure | low asset | medium asset | high asset | critical asset |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Source-like implementation | low | medium | high | critical |
| Readable names/structure | low | low | medium | high |

An associated asset whose value is omitted uses the existing manifest default, medium. Normal unassociated results use the registered baseline, not another asset's value. Known associations can lower or raise the baseline: this is deliberate contextual policy, not independent evidence that the artifact changed.

## Fallback and confidence

- Unknown rule: retain adapter severity; do not invent a class.
- Registered rule with a mismatched platform, undeclared asset ID, or invalid internal asset value: retain adapter severity and investigate consistency. An undeclared ID differs from a legitimate empty association (`asset_id=null`).
- Non-fail results remain info; open/error needs diagnosis, not escalation to an exposure problem.
- Confidence does not change the severity matrix. A critical/inferred result describes potentially critical consequences with uncertain evidence, not verified compromise. Do not hide the confidence or infer a level from severity.
- Changing the target, performance budget, server-side declaration or score does not reduce severity. Matched shipped exposure remains associated even when an asset declares server-side execution.

The Markdown summary names the severity policy; each fail entry includes a Severity basis explanation. Normal CLI/core scans normalize before statistics and export, so JSON severity/counts and SARIF levels derive from the same findings. SARIF does not gain severity_counts or numerical security-severity metadata. Detailed rationale is Markdown-only; it is not a new Finding or JSON field.

Direct adapter calls still return their baselines. A manually supplied ScanResult without recorded normalization is displayed as supplied severities, not as policy-normalized. The renderer does not secretly apply a second policy. Compare trends only for unchanged declarations and policy.

## Limits

These initial classes apply to implementation confidentiality. Do not extend them to credentials, personal data, licensing bypass, endpoints or other consequences by guessing from a title or level. Additional classes require explicit rule registration and review. The matrix is an initial policy, not calibrated loss estimation or an automatic release gate.

Read the rule-specific remediation guide for options and costs. A high severity does not authorize applying fixes, and 100 target-attainment points do not clear the exposure.

Maintainers: authoritative semantics are in `protocol/05-report-format.md` in the source repository and the core severity registry. Keep this bundled guide aligned when the policy changes; the repository is not required to interpret installed-skill results.

Library callers that modify findings or asset declarations must rerun normalization and summary calculation before exporting. Markdown suppresses recorded rationale when its decision inputs have changed; this guard does not recalculate results.

```

`skills/reshield/references/technical-details.md`:

```md
# Technical details

Read this when the user asks for the full technical report, CI or SARIF output, protection levels, confidence, scoring rules, or when validating ReShield itself. Developers asking "what should I fix" do not need any of it.

## CLI

The bundled CLI needs Python 3.11 or later and only the standard library. Replace `<skill-dir>` with this skill's installed directory; use `python3` if needed.

| Command | Use |
|---|---|
| `python "<skill-dir>/scripts/reshield_cli.py" detect "<build>"` | Identify the platform; JSON with `status` `detected`, `unknown` or `error` (exit code 2) |
| `... scan "<build>" --format summary` | Developer summary: actions grouped by fix, in plain language |
| `... scan "<build>" --format json` | Full data, including `actions` (the developer summary) and every finding |
| `... scan "<build>" --format markdown` | Full technical report, see [report-format.md](report-format.md) |
| `... scan "<build>" --format sarif` | GitHub, VS Code and CI |

Add `--manifest "<path>"` when `reshield.toml` is not in the build folder or its parents, and `--platform unity|godot|web` only when detection picked the wrong platform. Exit code 0 does not mean the build is protected; read the results.

## Result kinds

Each finding has a `kind`, following SARIF 2.1.0 `result.kind`:

- `fail`: exposure found at `level`.
- `pass`: protection confirmed for `level`.
- `open`: the build did not give enough information to decide. Never present it as protection.
- `error`: the check could not complete, for example a file could not be read.
- `notapplicable`: the rule does not apply to this build.

## Levels and confidence

Levels L0 to L5 are defined in [protection-levels.md](protection-levels.md). Each level carries a confidence: `observed` (seen in the build), `inferred` (heuristic, may be wrong) or `declared` (stated in `reshield.toml`, not verified). A level found by reading the source project instead of a build is a prediction and counts as `inferred`.

The Unity adapter in this release checks L0 and L1 only; L2 to L4 are listed in `summary.not_checked_levels`. Do not install analysis packages to fill this gap. Evidence mapping, L2 to L4 checks and their interface changes belong to the v0.2 proposal and are not released.

## Assets, completeness and scores

- Assets are declared in `reshield.toml`; see [asset-declaration.md](asset-declaration.md).
- `summary.complete` is false when any result is `open` or `error`. The overall level is then an upper bound and no score is given.
- The score (asset-target-v1) is the share of declared asset value weight that meets its target: critical 4, high 3, medium 2, low 1. It needs declared assets, a complete scan and an observed level for every asset; otherwise it is withheld. A score of 100 can reflect a low target, not strong protection.
- Severity follows severity-v1; see [severity-policy.md](severity-policy.md). Severity is a consequence category, not proof of exploitation.
- A declaration never hides observed exposure: a `server_side` asset whose code ships is reported at its observed level, not L5.

## Remediation catalog

Look up a `fail` result's exact `rule_id` in [remediation/README.md](remediation/README.md) for steps, costs and how to verify a fix. Keep runtime cost, engineering effort and compatibility risk separate. If guidance is missing or conflicts with the scanner's suggestion, say so instead of inventing it.

## Reports and exports

The full technical report follows [report-format.md](report-format.md). JSON and SARIF contain scanner data only; keep AI observations in the readable report or a separate note. Write report files outside the assessed build and confirm they exist before saying they were delivered.

## Validating ReShield

Read [validation.md](validation.md) when testing ReShield, comparing benchmark samples or preparing a branch integration. Merge branches only when the user explicitly asks.

```

`skills/reshield/references/validation.md`:

```md
# ReShield testing and validation

Use this guide to validate the skill, compare release samples, or prepare branch integration. An ordinary read-only assessment does not require obtaining the source repository or running developer tests first.

## Distinguish the validation goals

| Goal | What passing establishes | What it does not establish |
|---|---|---|
| Command and report consistency | Commands run and fields and unknown results follow the contract | The target meets its protection requirements |
| Sample detection | Observations from actual artifacts match the sample expectation | Support for every engine version or protection tool |
| Asset protection targets | An asset's level meets its target, with confidence and limitations stated | Reverse engineering is impossible or backend deployment is certified |

An unprotected Mono sample reported at L0 is successful detection, even when target_level=L2 and meets_target=false. Exit code 0, parseable JSON, or an empty findings list does not establish protection success.

## Fix samples and assessment boundaries

- Locate actual release artifacts using user-provided paths, branches, and existing benchmark documentation. Record source, artifacts, and expected results separately. Branch names and protection settings are not artifact evidence.
- Record missing release artifacts explicitly. Synthetic format samples exercise parser branches; they do not establish calibration against actual Unity releases.
- Operating-system virtual machines, Mono/CLR, IL2CPP, WebAssembly, and filenames containing VM do not establish L4 by themselves.
- Fix the target, manifest, scanner version, and expected assets. Write reports outside the build directory and compare artifact hashes and modification times before and after scanning.
- The scanner does not execute, load, modify, or upload targets. Executing an assessed program requires separate explicit authorization.

## Validate frozen-release reports

Generate a report with the bundled CLI, replacing the placeholder paths:

```text
python "<skill-dir>/scripts/reshield_cli.py" scan "<build-path>" --platform unity --manifest "<manifest-path>" --format json --output "<outside-build>/report.json"
```

Verify target, platforms, manifest, findings, and summary against the intended scope. Then check each asset's observed_level, confidence, status, and meets_target. Do not substitute the overall level or target level for an unknown asset result.

| Scenario | Required result |
|---|---|
| Mono with readable game IL | L0/observed; Release settings do not raise the level |
| IL2CPP with readable names | L1/observed; an unknown metadata version does not erase sufficient name-exposure evidence |
| Missing, damaged, or unreadable metadata | open/error; do not infer L2 |
| Unity L2-L4 | Listed in summary.not_checked_levels; do not claim verification |
| A server_side asset matches exposure evidence | Retain asset_id; observed L0/L1 takes precedence over declared L5 |
| Unmatched evidence or unrelated assets | Keep unmatched results at asset_id=null; do not copy exposure to unrelated assets |
| A complete scan finds no matching result for a server_side asset | The core may report L5/declared under existing rules; backend deployment remains unverified |
| A lower-level fail and a higher-level pass apply to the same asset | Use the lower exposed level; do not hide it with a higher result |
| Any applicable open/error result | The assessment is incomplete and unscored; global unknowns affect all assets, while local unknowns affect only the relevant assets |

## Validate target-attainment scoring

Scores use `asset-target-v1`. Compare numeric scores only with unchanged asset scope, values, targets, and policy.

- A score is the percentage of attained asset value weight (critical=4, high=3, medium=2, low=1), not absolute protection or recovery cost. Count each asset once, regardless of repeated findings.
- Every declared asset needs an available observed-confidence level and the assessment must be complete. Any unknown, inferred, or declared asset level withholds scoring. No declared assets also means no target-attainment score, even though an overall level may be available.
- An observed L0 sample below its L2 target is a successful detection but earns no attainment weight. The same observed L0 with target L0 can score 100 while its protection level remains L0.
- Check that the Markdown policy label, attained weight fraction, withheld reason, and critical-asset gaps agree with the JSON's existing score, asset results, and tool version. JSON has no additional policy or score-reason field. SARIF retains result details and the tool version, without a numeric score field.
- Missing evidence must never improve the score. Do not exclude unknown assets or treat inferred/declared target comparisons as verified passes to obtain a percentage.

v0.1 uses only the Python standard library. Evidence mapping and advanced Unity analysis are proposed for v0.2.

## Validate readable report structure

Read [report-format.md](report-format.md) before reviewing a readable assessment. Validate the rendered output, not only the presence of instructions:

- The summary comes first, followed by scoring basis, association/coverage limits, assets, fail findings, open/error diagnostics, confirmed passes and the final not-applicable rule line. Empty sections are omitted except the summary.
- A mixed-result report keeps fail, pass and open/error entries separate. Fail entries are ordered by severity. All not-applicable rule ids are listed even when the user did not ask about them separately.
- Preserve available line numbers and symbols for fail, pass and open/error locations. Missing fail evidence or remediation is explicitly identified rather than supplied by the report author.
- Association warnings cover every result kind, identify affected rules and invalid asset ids, and distinguish no available results from an asset made undetermined by open/error. Whole-build unknowns still affect all declared assets.
- When scoring is withheld, the summary explains why and still shows the complete declared weight, without claiming an attained numerator. Inferred/declared comparisons are unverified, not silently failed or counted as verified attainment.
- An empty result set explicitly establishes nothing about protection. A missing manifest permits the existing fail/pass overall-level fallback but no target-attainment score.
- Supplemental environment details, AI observations and next actions follow the canonical body in a labeled note; they are not claimed as scanner findings or JSON/SARIF contents.
- Render JSON, Markdown and SARIF from the same scan data to check consistency and ensure rendering does not mutate that data. Format tests do not validate detection accuracy against real releases.

## Maintainer regression tests

Use Python 3.11+ from the source repository root:

```text
python -B -S -m unittest discover -s tests -v
python -B -S -m unittest discover -s benchmarks/tests -v
```

- -S excludes site-packages, verifying that core tests do not require third-party packages.
- Real Mono benchmark tests run only when the corresponding artifacts exist. Report missing-artifact skips explicitly; synthetic samples are not substitutes for real-artifact validation.
- If sandbox temporary-directory permissions fail, set TEMP/TMP for the test process to a writable directory. Do not change the user's global settings.
- Cross-platform CI covers Windows, macOS, Linux, and Python 3.11-3.13. Local success does not establish success on other platforms.

`skills/reshield/scripts/reshield/actions.py`:

```py
"""Developer summary: what to fix, in plain language, grouped by fix.

Spec: protocol/05-report-format.md#developer-summary

Developers act on fixes, not on individual findings. Results that one fix
resolves are grouped into one action, ranked as "must", "should" or "check".
The technical report keeps every finding; this layer only reorganizes them.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from .models import UNDETERMINED_KINDS, AssetValue, Finding, Kind, Level, Severity, code_area
from .scanner import ScanResult

# What an analyst can get back at each level, in words a developer understands.
PLAIN_LEVEL = {
    Level.L0: "can be read almost like your source code",
    Level.L1: "can be decompiled with readable names and structure",
    Level.L2: "logic is readable but names are hidden",
    Level.L3: "logic is hard to follow without dedicated tools",
    Level.L4: "logic runs inside a custom virtual machine",
    Level.L5: "never ships to players",
}

PLAIN_CHECK = {
    Level.L2: "hidden names",
    Level.L3: "scrambled logic",
    Level.L4: "virtualized code",
}

# Why it matters, in words for someone who knows their engine but not security.
_EVERYDAY_WHY = {
    Level.L0: "anyone can open your release files with a free tool and read this code almost exactly as you wrote it.",
    Level.L1: "people can still see the names in your code, which shows them exactly where to look.",
    Level.L2: "people can still work out how this code behaves, even though the names are hidden.",
}
_RULE_WHY = {
    "RS-CORE-002": "you shipped files that are only meant for your own debugging, and they make your code much easier to read.",
    "RS-UNREAL-001": "anyone can unpack your packaged assets and Blueprints with a free tool.",
    "RS-WEB-002": "your original code, with your comments, ships with your release for anyone to read.",
}

_PLAIN_COST = {
    "none": "no runtime cost",
    "low": "little or no runtime cost",
    "moderate": "noticeable runtime cost on the protected code",
    "high": "high runtime cost on the protected code",
}

_IMPORTANT = (AssetValue.CRITICAL, AssetValue.HIGH)
_URGENT = (Severity.CRITICAL, Severity.HIGH)


@dataclass
class Action:
    priority: str  # "must", "should" or "check"
    title: str
    why: str
    assets: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # declared assets this action protects
    other_places: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # code outside declared assets
    files: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    cost: str | None = None
    after: str | None = None  # what the protection looks like after the fix
    steps: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    rule_ids: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    worst: int = 9  # lowest level in the group; worst first. Not exported.
    gravity: int = 9  # most severe finding in the group, 0 = critical. Not exported.
    label: str | None = None  # short name of an unchecked part, for the summary. Not exported.

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "priority": self.priority,
            "title": self.title,
            "why": self.why,
            "assets": self.assets,
            "other_places": self.other_places,
            "files": self.files,
            "cost": self.cost,
            "after": self.after,
            "steps": self.steps,
            "rule_ids": self.rule_ids,
        }


def _count(n: int, word: str, plural: str | None = None) -> str:
    return f"{n} {word if n == 1 else (plural or word + 's')}"


def _join_or(items: list[str]) -> str:
    return items[0] if len(items) == 1 else ", ".join(items[:-1]) + " or " + items[-1]


def _asset_name(asset_id: str) -> str:
    """Human-readable asset name: "combat-formulas" -> "combat formulas"."""
    return asset_id.replace("-", " ").replace("_", " ")


def _after(level: Level) -> str:
    if level is Level.L5:
        return "this code no longer ships to players"
    plain = PLAIN_LEVEL[level]
    still = "can still " + plain[4:] if plain.startswith("can ") else "still: " + plain
    return f"harder to copy, but your code {still}"


def _unique(items) -> list:
    return list(dict.fromkeys(i for i in items if i))


def _everyday_why(finding: Finding) -> str:
    if finding.rule_id == "RS-CORE-001":
        return finding.impact  # already written for a non-expert, with the masked key
    if finding.rule_id in _RULE_WHY:
        return _RULE_WHY[finding.rule_id]
    return _EVERYDAY_WHY.get(finding.level, f"your code {PLAIN_LEVEL[finding.level]}.")


def _is_must(finding: Finding, result: ScanResult) -> bool:
    asset = result.manifest.asset(finding.asset_id) if finding.asset_id else None
    if asset is not None:
        observed = next((a for a in result.summary.assets if a.asset_id == asset.id), None)
        misses = observed is not None and observed.meets_target is False
        return misses and asset.value in _IMPORTANT
    return finding.severity in _URGENT


def _fix_actions(result: ScanResult) -> list[Action]:
    groups: dict[str, list[Finding]] = {}
    for f in result.findings:
        if f.kind is Kind.FAIL:
            key = f.remediation[0].summary if f.remediation else f.rule_id
            groups.setdefault(key, []).append(f)

    actions = []
    for key, findings in groups.items():
        fix = next((f.remediation[0] for f in findings if f.remediation), None)
        worst = min(findings, key=lambda f: f.level)
        assets = _unique(f.asset_id for f in findings)
        actions.append(Action(
            priority="must" if any(_is_must(f, result) for f in findings) else "should",
            title=fix.summary if fix else findings[0].title,
            why=_everyday_why(worst),
            assets=assets,
            other_places=_unique(code_area(f.location.symbol) for f in findings if not f.asset_id),
            files=_unique(f.location.path for f in findings),
            cost=_PLAIN_COST.get(fix.performance_cost, fix.performance_cost) if fix else None,
            after=_after(fix.target_level) if fix else None,
            steps=list(fix.steps) if fix else [],
            rule_ids=_unique(f.rule_id for f in findings),
            worst=int(worst.level),
            gravity=min(_SEVERITY_ORDER.index(f.severity) for f in findings),
        ))
    return actions


def _check_actions(result: ScanResult) -> list[Action]:
    actions = []
    for f in result.findings:
        if f.kind in UNDETERMINED_KINDS:
            couldnt = "could not finish this check" if f.kind is Kind.ERROR else "could not tell from the build"
            actions.append(Action(
                priority="check",
                title=f"{f.title}: {couldnt}",
                why=f"{f.impact} Until this is checked, do not assume this part is protected.",
                assets=[f.asset_id] if f.asset_id else [],
                files=[f.location.path],
                steps=list(f.remediation[0].steps) if f.remediation else [],
                rule_ids=[f.rule_id],
                label=f.title if f.rule_id == "RS-CORE-004" else None,
            ))
    return actions


_ORDER = {"must": 0, "should": 1, "check": 2}
_SEVERITY_ORDER = list(Severity)


def developer_actions(result: ScanResult) -> list[Action]:
    actions = _fix_actions(result) + _check_actions(result)
    return sorted(actions, key=lambda a: (_ORDER[a.priority], a.gravity, a.worst))


def verdict(result: ScanResult, actions: list[Action]) -> str:
    if not result.findings:
        return "We could not check anything in these files, so we can't tell you whether your code is safe."
    must = [a for a in actions if a.priority == "must"]
    should = [a for a in actions if a.priority == "should"]
    if must:
        names = _unique(_asset_name(name) for action in must for name in action.assets)
        what = "Your " + " and ".join(names) if names else "Important parts of your code"
        plural = not names or len(names) > 1 or names[0].endswith("s")
        return f"{what} {'are' if plural else 'is'} easy to copy right now."
    if should:
        return "Nothing urgent, but a few changes would make your code harder to copy."
    if not result.summary.complete:
        return "We couldn't check everything, so we can't say your code is protected yet."
    return "We didn't find anything easy to copy. That's good, but it doesn't mean your code can't be copied."


# Types the engine or compiler generates; they are not the developer's code.
_GENERATED_PREFIXES = ("UnitySourceGenerated", "<")


def code_areas(result: ScanResult) -> list[str]:
    """Game code areas found in exposed results, to help the developer pick what to protect."""
    areas = _unique(code_area(f.location.symbol) for f in result.findings if f.kind is Kind.FAIL)
    # Squashed code has one- or two-letter names that mean nothing to the developer.
    return [a for a in areas if not a.startswith(_GENERATED_PREFIXES) and len(a) >= 3]


def _check_tip(why: str) -> str | None:
    """The setting that would make an unchecked part checkable, when the reason names one."""
    match = re.search(r"; ([^;]*?) to check it\b", why)
    return match.group(1)[0].upper() + match.group(1)[1:] + "." if match else None


def to_summary(result: ScanResult) -> str:
    """Render the developer summary: short, everyday words, at most three things to do."""
    actions = developer_actions(result)
    todo = [a for a in actions if a.priority in ("must", "should")]
    unchecked = [a for a in actions if a.priority == "check"]
    lines = ["# Before you release", "", f"**{verdict(result, actions)}**", ""]

    if todo:
        lines += ["Fix these first:" if len(todo) > 1 else "Fix this:", ""]
        for number, a in enumerate(todo[:3], 1):
            lines += [f"{number}. **{a.title}**"]
            if a.files:
                more = f" and {_count(len(a.files) - 1, 'other file')}" if len(a.files) > 1 else ""
                lines.append(f"   Where: `{a.files[0]}`{more}")
            lines.append(f"   Why: {a.why[0].upper()}{a.why[1:]}")
            if a.steps:
                lines.append(f"   Fix: {a.steps[0]}")
            lines.append("")
        if len(todo) > 3:
            lines += [f"There {'is' if len(todo) == 4 else 'are'} {_count(len(todo) - 3, 'more thing')} to look at after these.", ""]

    if unchecked:
        files = _unique(result.target.name if path in ("", ".") else path
                        for a in unchecked if not a.label for path in a.files)
        where = ""
        if files:
            more = f" and {_count(len(files) - 3, 'other')}" if len(files) > 3 else ""
            where = " (" + ", ".join(f"`{f}`" for f in files[:3]) + more + ")"
        lines += [f"We couldn't check {_count(len(unchecked), 'part')} of your release{where}. "
                  f"That doesn't mean {'it is' if len(unchecked) == 1 else 'they are'} safe.", ""]
        # Some parts can be checked after one setting change; adapters end those reasons with "to check it".
        for tip in _unique(_check_tip(a.why) for a in unchecked if not a.label):
            if tip:
                lines += [f"To check it: {tip}", ""]
        for a in (a for a in unchecked if a.label):
            hint = a.why.split("Usually ", 1)[-1].split(" Until this", 1)[0]
            lines += [f"- **{a.label}** in `{a.files[0]}`: {hint}", f"  Fix: {a.steps[0]}", ""]

    protected = [f for f in result.findings if f.rule_id == "RS-CORE-005"]
    if protected:
        shown = ", ".join(f"{f.title.removesuffix(' protection')} in `{f.location.path}`" for f in protected[:3])
        more = f" and {len(protected) - 3} more" if len(protected) > 3 else ""
        lines += [f"Protection we found: {shown}{more}. Keep it on, and make sure it covers your important code.", ""]

    if not result.manifest.assets:
        areas = code_areas(result)[:5]
        if areas:
            lines += ["Which part of your code matters most to you (for example payments, game rules or license checks)? We found: " + ", ".join(f"`{a}`" for a in areas), ""]

    if todo:
        lines.append("Not sure how? Ask your AI assistant: \"fix step 1 for me\".")
    lines.append("For the full technical report, run the scan with `--format markdown`.")
    return "\n".join(lines)

```

`skills/reshield/scripts/reshield/adapters/__init__.py`:

```py
"""Adapter registry. Register new platforms in ADAPTERS."""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

from .base import Adapter, ScanContext
from .android import AndroidAdapter
from .godot import GodotAdapter
from .native import NativeAdapter
from .unity import UnityAdapter
from .unreal import UnrealAdapter
from .web import WebAdapter

# Order matters: more specific platforms first, since a Unity or Godot
# build folder can also contain loose .js files. Native is the fallback for
# any other program, so it comes last.
ADAPTERS: list[Adapter] = [UnityAdapter(), GodotAdapter(), UnrealAdapter(), AndroidAdapter(), WebAdapter(), NativeAdapter()]

# Adapters that also run when the first match is found, because that technology ships
# its own code as native binaries (Unreal C++, Android .so libraries).
COMPANIONS = {"unreal": ("native",), "android": ("native",)}


def get_adapter(name: str) -> Adapter:
    for adapter in ADAPTERS:
        if adapter.name == name:
            return adapter
    known = ", ".join(a.name for a in ADAPTERS)
    raise KeyError(f"Unknown platform '{name}'. Known platforms: {known}")


def detect_adapters(target: Path) -> list[Adapter]:
    """Return the first adapter that recognizes the target plus its companions, or an empty list."""
    for adapter in ADAPTERS:
        if adapter.detect(target):
            extra = [get_adapter(name) for name in COMPANIONS.get(adapter.name, ())]
            return [adapter] + [a for a in extra if a.detect(target)]
    return []


__all__ = ["ADAPTERS", "Adapter", "ScanContext", "detect_adapters", "get_adapter"]

```

`skills/reshield/scripts/reshield/adapters/android.py`:

```py
"""Android Java and Kotlin code in DEX files (extracted .apk or .aab).

Checks whether the app's own classes keep readable names, which tools such as jadx
turn back into near-source Java, or were renamed by R8/ProGuard.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import struct
from pathlib import Path

from ..models import Confidence, Evidence, Finding, Kind, Level, Location, Remediation, Severity
from .base import Adapter, ScanContext

MAX_DEX = 64
MAX_DEX_SIZE = 256 * 1024 * 1024
# Libraries and runtimes: not the developer's own code.
_LIBRARY_PREFIXES = (
    "Landroid/", "Landroidx/", "Ljava/", "Ljavax/", "Lkotlin/", "Lkotlinx/", "Lcom/google/", "Lcom/android/",
    "Lcom/unity3d/", "Lcom/facebook/", "Lio/flutter/", "Lorg/", "Lokhttp3/", "Lokio/", "Lretrofit2/",
    "Ldagger/", "Lj$/", "Lcom/squareup/", "Lbitter/", "Lcom/epicgames/", "Lcom/godot/", "Lorg/godotengine/",
)


def _uleb128(data: bytes, pos: int) -> tuple[int, int]:
    result = shift = 0
    while True:
        byte = data[pos]
        pos += 1
        result |= (byte & 0x7F) << shift
        if byte < 0x80:
            return result, pos
        shift += 7


def dex_classes(data: bytes) -> list[str]:
    """Descriptors of the classes defined in a DEX file, for example "Lcom/game/Combat;"."""
    if not data.startswith(b"dex\n") or len(data) < 0x70:
        raise ValueError("not a DEX file")
    string_ids_off = struct.unpack_from("<I", data, 0x3C)[0]
    type_ids_size, type_ids_off = struct.unpack_from("<II", data, 0x40)
    class_defs_size, class_defs_off = struct.unpack_from("<II", data, 0x60)
    if class_defs_size > 500_000 or class_defs_off + class_defs_size * 32 > len(data):
        raise ValueError("class definitions out of range")
    names = []
    for i in range(class_defs_size):
        type_index = struct.unpack_from("<I", data, class_defs_off + i * 32)[0]
        if type_index >= type_ids_size:
            raise ValueError("type index out of range")
        string_index = struct.unpack_from("<I", data, type_ids_off + type_index * 4)[0]
        string_off = struct.unpack_from("<I", data, string_ids_off + string_index * 4)[0]
        _, start = _uleb128(data, string_off)
        end = data.index(b"\0", start)
        names.append(data[start:end].decode("utf-8", "replace"))
    return names


def _generated(descriptor: str) -> bool:
    """Build-generated classes (resource ids, build constants) are not the developer's logic."""
    outer = descriptor.rstrip(";").rsplit("/", 1)[-1].split("$")[0]
    return outer in ("R", "BuildConfig", "Manifest")


def _simple_name(descriptor: str) -> str:
    return descriptor.rstrip(";").rsplit("/", 1)[-1].split("$")[-1]


def _dotted(descriptor: str) -> str:
    return descriptor[1:].rstrip(";").replace("/", ".")


def _r8_fix() -> Remediation:
    return Remediation("Turn on R8 to shrink and rename your Java and Kotlin code", Level.L2, "none", [
        "In the app's build.gradle, set minifyEnabled true (isMinifyEnabled = true in Kotlin scripts) for the release build type.",
        "Keep only the names that reflection, JSON parsing or native code need, with keep rules in proguard-rules.pro.",
        "Build the release again, test it, and scan; most class names should now be short letters.",
    ])


class AndroidAdapter(Adapter):
    name = "android"
    display_name = "Android (Java and Kotlin)"
    checked_levels = frozenset({Level.L0, Level.L2})

    def detect(self, target: Path) -> bool:
        return target.is_dir() and any(target.glob("classes*.dex"))

    def scan(self, context: ScanContext) -> list[Finding]:
        app_classes: list[str] = []
        files = sorted(context.target.glob("classes*.dex"))[:MAX_DEX]
        for path in files:
            if path.stat().st_size > MAX_DEX_SIZE:
                return [self._open(path.name, "DEX file larger than the inspection limit")]
            try:
                classes = dex_classes(path.read_bytes())
            except (ValueError, IndexError, struct.error) as error:
                return [self._open(path.name, str(error))]
            app_classes += [c for c in classes if not c.startswith(_LIBRARY_PREFIXES) and not _generated(c)]
        where = files[0].name if files else "."
        if not app_classes:
            return [Finding("RS-ANDROID-001", "Java and Kotlin class names", self.name, Severity.INFO, None,
                            Location(where), "Only library classes were found; there is no app Java or Kotlin code to check.",
                            kind=Kind.NOT_APPLICABLE)]
        short = [c for c in app_classes if len(_simple_name(c)) <= 2]
        ratio = len(short) / len(app_classes)
        evidence = [Evidence(f"{len(app_classes)} app classes, {len(short)} with renamed (short) names", where)]
        if ratio >= 0.5:
            return [Finding("RS-ANDROID-001", "Java and Kotlin class names", self.name, Severity.INFO, Level.L2,
                            Location(where), "Most class names were renamed, so the code no longer says what it does. "
                            "The logic itself can still be decompiled.", confidence=Confidence.INFERRED, kind=Kind.PASS,
                            evidence=evidence)]
        readable = [_dotted(c) for c in app_classes if len(_simple_name(c)) > 2]
        groups: dict[str | None, str] = {}
        for name in readable:
            asset = context.manifest.match_asset(symbol=name)
            groups.setdefault(asset.id if asset else None, name)
        return [Finding(
            "RS-ANDROID-001", "Java and Kotlin class names", self.name, Severity.HIGH, Level.L0,
            Location(where, symbol=symbol), "Free tools such as jadx turn this app back into near-source Java with your original names.",
            confidence=Confidence.OBSERVED, evidence=evidence + [Evidence(f"For example {symbol}", where)],
            remediation=[_r8_fix()], asset_id=asset_id,
        ) for asset_id, symbol in groups.items()]

    def _open(self, where: str, reason: str) -> Finding:
        return Finding("RS-ANDROID-001", "Java and Kotlin class names", self.name, Severity.INFO, None, Location(where),
                       f"Could not read the DEX file: {reason}. Missing evidence does not mean protection.", kind=Kind.OPEN)

```

`skills/reshield/scripts/reshield/adapters/base.py`:

```py
"""Adapter interface: the only platform-specific layer in ReShield.

Spec: protocol/adapter-interface.md

An adapter recognizes one kind of build and turns what it observes into
`Finding` objects. Scoring, reporting and remediation priorities live in the
core, so a new platform should only need a new adapter.

Rules for adapters:
- Read-only. Never modify, execute or upload the target.
- Only report what was observed; put the observation in `Finding.evidence`.
- Paths in `Location` are relative to `ScanContext.target`.
- Link each finding to an asset with `context.manifest.match_asset(symbol=..., path=...)`.
- Report every rule you evaluate, with a `Finding.kind`: "fail" for exposure,
  "pass" for confirmed protection, "open" when the build does not give enough
  information, "error" when evaluation could not complete, "notapplicable" when
  the rule does not apply. An empty list must never stand for "could not tell".
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path

from ..manifest import Manifest
from ..models import Finding, Level


@dataclass
class ScanContext:
    """Frozen scan inputs: target and manifest. New inputs need an approved protocol version."""

    target: Path
    manifest: Manifest


class Adapter(ABC):
    #: Stable identifier used on the command line and in rule ids.
    name: str
    #: Human-readable platform name for reports.
    display_name: str
    #: Levels whose criteria this adapter evaluates. The others are reported as
    #: "not checked" (XCCDF notchecked). L5 comes from the manifest, not the adapter.
    checked_levels: frozenset[Level] = frozenset()

    @abstractmethod
    def detect(self, target: Path) -> bool:
        """Return True if `target` looks like a build for this platform."""

    @abstractmethod
    def scan(self, context: ScanContext) -> list[Finding]:
        """Inspect the build and return findings."""

```

`skills/reshield/scripts/reshield/adapters/godot.py`:

```py
"""Translate read-only Godot artifact evidence into existing Findings; the core owns scoring and output."""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

from ..models import Confidence, Evidence, Finding, Kind, Level, Location, Remediation, Severity
from .base import Adapter, ScanContext
from .godot_pack import MAX_DIRECTORY_ENTRIES, inspect_pack


# Report text and tokens separately so mixed exports retain the lower-level text exposure.
_RULES = {
    "text": ("RS-GODOT-001", "Source-like Godot code exposure"),
    "tokens": ("RS-GODOT-002", "Godot binary token exposure"),
}


def _remediation(representation: str) -> Remediation:
    """Build a Remediation for representation: str; its target is an intention, not verified protection."""
    if representation == "text":
        return Remediation(
            "Export GDScript as binary tokens", Level.L1, "low",
            ["In Godot: Project > Export > choose your preset > Script tab > GDScript Export Mode, choose Binary tokens.",
             "Rebuild to a clean output and verify behavior, loading performance, and absence of original script text.",
             "Tokens still expose recoverable names and logic; this change targets L1, not L2."],
        )
    return Remediation(
        "Plan pack encryption for sensitive scripts", Level.L2, "unknown; measure startup and resource loading",
        ["Agree on encryption scope and key handling before changing the source project or export templates.",
         "Use export templates built with the encryption key; account for plaintext copies and any C# assemblies.",
         "Rebuild and verify behavior and exposure. This adapter cannot verify L2; an encrypted flag or open result is not proof of protection."],
    )


class GodotAdapter(Adapter):
    """Godot entry point. No constructor arguments; detect returns bool and scan returns list[Finding]."""

    name = "godot"
    display_name = "Godot"
    # L2 and higher need additional evidence; encryption flags alone cannot establish attainment.
    checked_levels = frozenset({Level.L0, Level.L1})

    def detect(self, target: Path) -> bool:
        """Check target: Path for PCK candidates and return bool without parsing binaries during detection."""
        if target.is_file():
            return target.suffix.casefold() == ".pck"
        if target.is_dir():
            for index, child in enumerate(target.iterdir()):
                if index >= MAX_DIRECTORY_ENTRIES:
                    return False
                if child.suffix.casefold() == ".pck":
                    return True
        return False

    def scan(self, context: ScanContext) -> list[Finding]:
        """Convert context: ScanContext observations to list[Finding]; declarations never exclude shipped exposure."""
        inspection = inspect_pack(context.target)
        findings = []
        artifacts = list(dict.fromkeys([*inspection.artifacts, *(i.artifact for i in inspection.issues)])) or ["."]
        for artifact in artifacts:
            for representation, (rule, title) in _RULES.items():
                applicable = []
                for exposure in inspection.exposures:
                    if exposure.artifact != artifact or exposure.representation != representation:
                        continue
                    asset = context.manifest.match_asset(symbol=exposure.resource, path=exposure.resource.removeprefix("res://"))
                    applicable.append(Finding(
                        rule_id=rule, title=title, platform=self.name,
                        severity=Severity.HIGH if representation == "text" else Severity.MEDIUM,
                        level=Level.L0 if representation == "text" else Level.L1,
                        location=Location(path=artifact, symbol=exposure.resource),
                        impact=("Original script text is readable, so the shipped logic can be inspected and copied."
                                if representation == "text" else
                                "Unencrypted binary tokens retain script names and structure and can be analyzed to recover logic."),
                        confidence=Confidence.OBSERVED, evidence=exposure.evidence,
                        remediation=[_remediation(representation)], asset_id=asset.id if asset else None,
                    ))
                for issue in inspection.issues:
                    if issue.artifact != artifact or issue.representation not in (None, representation):
                        continue
                    # Use observed resource paths for unknown results too, keeping locatable limitations scoped to their assets.
                    asset = context.manifest.match_asset(symbol=issue.resource, path=issue.resource.removeprefix("res://")) if issue.resource else None
                    applicable.append(Finding(
                        rule_id=rule, title=title, platform=self.name, severity=Severity.INFO, level=None,
                        location=Location(path=artifact, symbol=issue.resource),
                        impact=f"Godot could not determine: {issue.reason}. Missing evidence does not mean protection.",
                        evidence=[Evidence(issue.reason, artifact)], asset_id=asset.id if asset else None,
                        kind=Kind.ERROR if issue.error else Kind.OPEN,
                    ))
                # Absence in an enumerated directory means not applicable, never a pass or a higher level.
                findings.extend(applicable or [Finding(
                    rule_id=rule, title=title, platform=self.name, severity=Severity.INFO, level=None,
                    location=Location(path=artifact), kind=Kind.NOT_APPLICABLE,
                    impact=f"No {representation} script representation applies in this inspected standalone PCK. "
                           "Embedded packs, compressed tokens, C#, GDExtension and L2-L4 protection are not verified.",
                    evidence=[Evidence("Supported PCK directory was enumerated; this representation was absent", artifact)],
                )])
        return findings

```

`skills/reshield/scripts/reshield/adapters/godot_pack.py`:

```py
"""Bounded read-only Godot PCK evidence reader; scoring, remediation and report formats belong elsewhere."""

from __future__ import annotations

import configparser
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path, PureWindowsPath
import stat
import struct

from ..models import Evidence
from .godot_script import UnsupportedScript, text_script, token_script


# Apply limits across the entire scan so multiple packs cannot bypass per-file resource budgets.
MAX_READ_BYTES = 64 * 1024 * 1024
MAX_ENTRY_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024
MAX_ENTRIES = 10000
MAX_PACKS = 32
MAX_DIRECTORY_ENTRIES = 4096
# Only recognize native or managed code candidates; never load them or infer protection from extensions.
_OTHER_CODE_SUFFIXES = (".dll", ".cs", ".csproj", ".gdextension", ".gde", ".so", ".dylib", ".a", ".wasm")


@dataclass
class ResourceExposure:
    """Verified exposure: artifact/resource/representation are str; evidence is list[Evidence]."""

    artifact: str
    resource: str
    representation: str
    evidence: list[Evidence]


@dataclass
class InspectionIssue:
    """Inspection limitation: artifact/reason are str, representation/resource are str | None, error is bool."""

    artifact: str
    reason: str
    representation: str | None = None
    resource: str | None = None
    error: bool = False


@dataclass
class PackInspection:
    """Inspection output: lists of exposures, unknown/error observations and inspected relative artifact paths."""

    exposures: list[ResourceExposure] = field(default_factory=list)
    issues: list[InspectionIssue] = field(default_factory=list)
    artifacts: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)


@dataclass
class _Budget:
    """Shared scan usage: read/entries are int counters that prevent aggregate overuse across packs."""

    read: int = 0
    entries: int = 0


@dataclass
class _Entry:
    """Directory entry: name is str, offset/size/flags are int and digest is bytes from the assessed directory."""

    name: str
    offset: int
    size: int
    flags: int
    digest: bytes


def _safe_file(path: Path) -> None:
    """Check path: Path is a regular file or directory; return None, rejecting links and special devices."""
    info = path.lstat()
    if (path.is_symlink() or getattr(info, "st_file_attributes", 0) & stat.FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT
            or not (stat.S_ISREG(info.st_mode) or stat.S_ISDIR(info.st_mode))):
        raise UnsupportedScript("Symbolic links, reparse points and special files are not inspected")


def _resource(name: str) -> str:
    """Normalize name: str to an unambiguous pack-relative str; reject traversal and host paths."""
    # Untrusted remap JSON literals may be numbers, collections or null; reject non-strings first.
    if not isinstance(name, str):
        raise UnsupportedScript("PCK resource identity must be a string")
    name = name.removeprefix("res://")
    if (not name or not name.isprintable() or "\\" in name or ":" in name
            or PureWindowsPath(name).drive or any(part in ("", ".", "..") for part in name.split("/"))):
        raise UnsupportedScript("Unsafe or ambiguous PCK resource path")
    return name


class _Reader:
    """Bounded binary reader: stream is a read-only file and budget: _Budget is shared across packs."""

    def __init__(self, stream, budget: _Budget):
        """Store stream and budget: _Budget; return None. Read size from the same descriptor to reduce races."""
        self.stream = stream
        self.budget = budget
        self.size = os.fstat(stream.fileno()).st_size
        self.position = 0
        self.format_description = ""

    def take(self, size: int) -> bytes:
        """Read size: int bytes and return bytes; check budgets and file bounds before allocating."""
        if size < 0 or self.position + size > self.size or self.budget.read + size > MAX_READ_BYTES:
            raise UnsupportedScript("PCK is truncated, out of bounds, or exceeds the shared read limit")
        self.budget.read += size
        self.stream.seek(self.position)
        data = self.stream.read(size)
        if len(data) != size:
            raise UnsupportedScript("PCK changed or was truncated while reading")
        self.position += size
        return data

    def number(self, fmt: str = "<I") -> int:
        """Read one number using fmt: str and return int; the default is a little-endian uint32."""
        return struct.unpack(fmt, self.take(struct.calcsize(fmt)))[0]


def _directory(reader: _Reader) -> list[_Entry]:
    """Read the complete directory from reader: _Reader; return list[_Entry] without reading or executing payloads."""
    if reader.take(4) != b"GDPC":
        raise UnsupportedScript("No standalone PCK header; embedded packs are not inspected")
    version = reader.number()
    engine = tuple(reader.number() for _ in range(3))
    flags, base = reader.number(), reader.number("<Q")
    if version not in (2, 3):
        raise UnsupportedScript("Unsupported PCK format version")
    # Format and engine versions are observations, not independent evidence of protection.
    reader.format_description = f"PCK format {version}, Godot {'.'.join(map(str, engine))}"
    if flags & ~2:
        raise UnsupportedScript("Encrypted directory, sparse bundle or unsupported pack flags")
    header_end = 104 if version == 3 else 96
    if version == 3:
        directory = reader.number("<Q")
        if directory < header_end:
            raise UnsupportedScript("PCK directory overlaps the header")
        reader.position = directory
    else:
        reader.take(64)
        directory = reader.position
    count = reader.number()
    reader.budget.entries += count
    if reader.budget.entries > MAX_ENTRIES:
        raise UnsupportedScript("PCK entry count exceeds the shared inspection limit")
    entries, names = [], set()
    for _ in range(count):
        length = reader.number()
        if not 1 <= length <= 4096:
            raise UnsupportedScript("PCK path length exceeds the inspection limit")
        name = _resource(reader.take(length).rstrip(b"\0").decode("utf-8"))
        if name in names:
            raise UnsupportedScript("Duplicate normalized PCK resource name")
        names.add(name)
        offset, size = reader.number("<Q"), reader.number("<Q")
        digest, entry_flags = reader.take(16), reader.number()
        entries.append(_Entry(name, base + offset, size, entry_flags, digest))
    directory_end = reader.position
    for entry in entries:
        # Removal records have no readable payload; report other flags per entry to preserve independent exposure.
        if entry.flags:
            continue
        end = entry.offset + entry.size
        if (entry.offset < header_end or end > reader.size
                or (entry.size and entry.offset < directory_end and end > directory)):
            raise UnsupportedScript("PCK payload overlaps metadata or extends outside the file")
    return entries


def _payload(reader: _Reader, entry: _Entry) -> bytes:
    """Read entry: _Entry from reader: _Reader and return verified bytes; MD5 checks integrity only."""
    if entry.flags:
        raise UnsupportedScript("Encrypted, removed or unsupported entry flags prevent inspection")
    if entry.size > MAX_ENTRY_BYTES:
        raise UnsupportedScript("Relevant PCK entry exceeds the inspection size limit")
    reader.position = entry.offset
    data = reader.take(entry.size)
    if hashlib.md5(data, usedforsecurity=False).digest() != entry.digest:
        raise UnsupportedScript("PCK entry hash does not match its payload")
    return data


def _remaps(reader: _Reader, entries: dict[str, _Entry], artifact: str, result: PackInspection) -> dict[str, str]:
    """Validate remaps in reader/entries, annotate result for artifact: str, and return a destination-to-source dict[str, str]."""
    candidates: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    for name, entry in entries.items():
        if not name.casefold().endswith(".gd.remap"):
            continue
        try:
            if entry.size > 64 * 1024:
                raise UnsupportedScript("Script remap exceeds the inspection size limit")
            # Godot has no INI DEFAULT inheritance or key folding; accept only explicit standard export structure.
            config = configparser.ConfigParser(interpolation=None, delimiters=("=",), empty_lines_in_values=False)
            config.optionxform = str
            config.read_string(_payload(reader, entry).decode("utf-8-sig"))
            if config.defaults() or config.sections() != ["remap"] or set(config["remap"]) != {"path"}:
                raise UnsupportedScript("Remap must contain only an explicit case-sensitive path in [remap]")
            literal = config["remap"]["path"].strip()
            if not literal.startswith('"') or not literal.endswith('"') or "\n" in literal:
                raise UnsupportedScript("Remap path must be a quoted resource string")
            target = _resource(json.loads(literal))
            if not target.casefold().endswith(".gdc") or target not in entries:
                raise UnsupportedScript("Remap destination is missing or is not a supported token resource")
            candidates.setdefault(target, []).append(name[:-6])
        except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError, RecursionError, configparser.Error) as error:
            result.issues.append(InspectionIssue(artifact, f"Invalid script remap: {str(error)[:256]}", "tokens", "res://" + name))
    mappings = {}
    for target, sources in candidates.items():
        if len(sources) != 1:
            result.issues.append(InspectionIssue(artifact, "Conflicting script remaps; original identity is unknown", "tokens"))
        else:
            mappings[target] = sources[0]
    return mappings


def _inspect_file(path: Path, artifact: str, budget: _Budget, result: PackInspection) -> None:
    """Inspect path: Path read-only and append artifact-scoped evidence to result: PackInspection; share budget and return None."""
    _safe_file(path)
    with path.open("rb") as stream:
        reader = _Reader(stream, budget)
        entries = {entry.name: entry for entry in _directory(reader)}
        mappings = _remaps(reader, entries, artifact, result)
        for name, entry in entries.items():
            # Preserve identity case, but classify formats case-insensitively so case differences cannot hide exposure.
            lower_name = name.casefold()
            representation = "text" if lower_name.endswith(".gd") else "tokens" if lower_name.endswith(".gdc") else None
            relevant = representation or lower_name.endswith(_OTHER_CODE_SUFFIXES) or entry.flags
            if not relevant:
                continue
            resource = "res://" + mappings.get(name, name)
            try:
                data = _payload(reader, entry)
                if representation is None:
                    raise UnsupportedScript("Managed code, native code or encrypted scripts are not inspected")
                (text_script if representation == "text" else token_script)(data)
                evidence = Evidence(
                    f"{reader.format_description}; unencrypted entry {name}: hash and {representation} structure verified"
                    + (f"; verified remap to {resource}" if name in mappings else ""), artifact)
                result.exposures.append(ResourceExposure(artifact, resource, representation, [evidence]))
            except (UnsupportedScript, UnicodeError, struct.error) as error:
                result.issues.append(InspectionIssue(artifact, str(error), representation, resource))


def inspect_pack(target: Path) -> PackInspection:
    """Inspect standalone PCKs in target: Path and return PackInspection; missing evidence never means protection."""
    result, budget = PackInspection(), _Budget()
    try:
        _safe_file(target)
        candidates = []
        if target.is_file():
            candidates = [(target, ".")]
        else:
            children = []
            for child in target.iterdir():
                children.append(child)
                if len(children) > MAX_DIRECTORY_ENTRIES:
                    raise UnsupportedScript("Build directory exceeds the inspection entry limit")
            for child in sorted(children):
                if child.suffix.casefold() == ".pck":
                    candidates.append((child, child.name))
                elif child.suffix.casefold() in _OTHER_CODE_SUFFIXES or child.name.casefold() in (".mono", ".godot"):
                    result.issues.append(InspectionIssue(child.name, "Possible external managed or native code is not inspected"))
        if not candidates:
            raise UnsupportedScript("No standalone PCK found; embedded packs and other export layouts are not inspected")
        if len(candidates) > MAX_PACKS:
            raise UnsupportedScript("Build exceeds the PCK count inspection limit")
        for path, artifact in candidates:
            result.artifacts.append(artifact)
            try:
                _inspect_file(path, artifact, budget, result)
            except OSError:
                # Do not export host exception messages, which may leak absolute paths into shareable reports.
                result.issues.append(InspectionIssue(artifact, "PCK could not be read because of an I/O or access error", error=True))
            except (UnsupportedScript, UnicodeError, struct.error) as error:
                result.issues.append(InspectionIssue(artifact, str(error)))
    except OSError:
        result.issues.append(InspectionIssue(".", "Target could not be read because of an I/O or access error", error=True))
    except UnsupportedScript as error:
        result.issues.append(InspectionIssue(".", str(error)))
    return result

```

`skills/reshield/scripts/reshield/adapters/godot_script.py`:

```py
"""Read-only validation of Godot 4.5.1 script representations; never compile, reconstruct or execute scripts."""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import struct


class UnsupportedScript(ValueError):
    """An evidence or format limitation; accepts a str reason for the caller to report as an open result."""


class _Cursor:
    """Bounded memory cursor: data: bytes is its only source; reads return bytes or int."""

    def __init__(self, data: bytes):
        """Store data: bytes at position zero to prevent unchecked slices; return None."""
        self.data = data
        self.position = 0

    def take(self, size: int) -> bytes:
        """Read size: int bytes and return bytes; truncated data must stop classification."""
        if size < 0 or self.position + size > len(self.data):
            raise UnsupportedScript("Script structure is truncated or out of bounds")
        start = self.position
        self.position += size
        return self.data[start:self.position]

    def number(self) -> int:
        """Return a little-endian uint32 using a fixed width for host-independent parsing."""
        return struct.unpack("<I", self.take(4))[0]


def text_script(data: bytes) -> None:
    """Validate data: bytes as readable GDScript; return None on success or raise an evidence limitation."""
    text = data.decode("utf-8-sig")
    # Recognizable structure establishes text exposure, not Godot syntax validity or correct behavior.
    if any(ord(char) < 32 and char not in "\r\n\t" for char in text):
        raise UnsupportedScript("Text script contains binary control characters")
    # Use horizontal whitespace only; line starts with cross-line \s* would cause quadratic backtracking.
    pattern = r"(?m)^[ \t]*(?:(?:static[ \t]+)?func[ \t]+[^\W\d]\w*[ \t]*\(|extends[ \t]+[^\s#]+|class_name[ \t]+[^\W\d]\w*|(?:var|const|signal)[ \t]+[^\W\d]\w*)"
    if not re.search(pattern, text):
        raise UnsupportedScript("Readable entry has no recognizable GDScript declaration")


def _constant(cursor: _Cursor) -> None:
    """Skip a supported value constant in cursor: _Cursor; return None without constructing objects."""
    header = cursor.number()
    kind = header & 0xFF
    # Only scalar/string constants are supported initially; unknown Variant lengths must never be guessed.
    if kind in (0, 1, 4, 21) and header != kind:
        raise UnsupportedScript("Unsupported Variant constant flags")
    if kind == 0:
        return
    if kind == 1:
        if cursor.number() not in (0, 1):
            raise UnsupportedScript("Invalid boolean constant")
    elif kind in (2, 3):
        if header & ~0x100FF:
            raise UnsupportedScript("Unsupported numeric constant flags")
        cursor.take(8 if header & 0x10000 else 4)
    elif kind in (4, 21):
        length = cursor.number()
        cursor.take(length).decode("utf-8")
        cursor.take(-length % 4)
    else:
        raise UnsupportedScript("Unsupported Variant constant type; no objects are decoded")


def token_script(data: bytes) -> None:
    """Validate uncompressed tokenizer v101 in data: bytes; return None on success or raise a format limitation."""
    cursor = _Cursor(data)
    if cursor.take(4) != b"GDSC" or cursor.number() != 101:
        raise UnsupportedScript("Unsupported GDSC tokenizer version; expected 101")
    if cursor.number() != 0:
        raise UnsupportedScript("Compressed GDSC tokens are not inspected")
    identifiers, constants, lines, tokens = (cursor.number() for _ in range(4))
    if max(identifiers, constants, lines, tokens) > 100000 or not tokens or lines > tokens:
        raise UnsupportedScript("Token counts are invalid or exceed the inspection limit")
    names = []
    for _ in range(identifiers):
        length = cursor.number()
        if not 1 <= length <= 4096:
            raise UnsupportedScript("Identifier length exceeds the inspection limit")
        # The official tokenizer XOR-encodes UTF-32 identifiers; raw bytes are not meaningful names.
        name = bytes(value ^ 0xB6 for value in cursor.take(length * 4)).decode("utf-32-le")
        if not (name.isidentifier() or (name.startswith("@") and name[1:].isidentifier())):
            raise UnsupportedScript("Invalid tokenizer identifier")
        names.append(name)
    for _ in range(constants):
        _constant(cursor)
    maps = []
    for _ in range(2):
        indexes = set()
        for _ in range(lines):
            index, value = cursor.number(), cursor.number()
            if index >= tokens or not value or index in indexes:
                raise UnsupportedScript("Invalid token line or column mapping")
            indexes.add(index)
        maps.append(indexes)
    if maps[0] != maps[1]:
        raise UnsupportedScript("Token line and column mappings disagree")
    has_declaration = False
    has_identifier = False
    for _ in range(tokens):
        first = cursor.take(1)[0]
        encoded = struct.unpack("<I", bytes([first]) + cursor.take(3))[0] if first & 0x80 else first
        kind, index = encoded & 0x7F, encoded >> 8
        line = cursor.number()
        # Tokenizer v101 ERROR=98 and EOF=99 do not belong in a normal exported token sequence.
        if kind >= 98 or not line:
            raise UnsupportedScript("Invalid token type or source line")
        if kind in (1, 2):
            if index >= identifiers:
                raise UnsupportedScript("Token identifier index is out of bounds")
            # The shared table stores full annotation names; validate each name against its actual token kind.
            name = names[index]
            if (kind == 1 and not name.startswith("@")) or (kind == 2 and not name.isidentifier()):
                raise UnsupportedScript("Identifier does not match its annotation or identifier token kind")
            has_identifier = True
        elif kind == 3:
            if index >= constants:
                raise UnsupportedScript("Token constant index is out of bounds")
        elif index:
            raise UnsupportedScript("Unexpected token payload")
        has_declaration |= kind in (55, 56, 57, 59, 60, 66, 70)
    if cursor.position != len(data) or not has_declaration or not has_identifier:
        raise UnsupportedScript("Token stream has trailing data or no recognizable declaration")

```

`skills/reshield/scripts/reshield/adapters/native.py`:

```py
"""Native and .NET programs: the fallback adapter for any desktop or server build.

Covers C, C++, Rust, Go, Swift and Objective-C binaries (PE, ELF, Mach-O) and any
.NET assembly (WPF, WinForms, MAUI, MonoGame, FNA, Stride, console apps).
Read-only: files are opened with "rb", never loaded or executed.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import re
import struct
from pathlib import Path

from ..models import Confidence, Evidence, Finding, Kind, Level, Location, Remediation, Severity, code_area
from .base import Adapter, ScanContext
from .unity_layout import _Binary, _managed_symbols, _pe, _Uncertain

MAX_BINARIES = 300
MAX_SIZE = 256 * 1024 * 1024
MAX_DEPTH = 3
HEAD = 1024 * 1024  # bytes read for marker searches in Mach-O load commands

_UNIVERSAL = b"\xca\xfe\xba\xbe"
_MAGICS = (b"MZ", b"\x7fELF", b"\xcf\xfa\xed\xfe", b"\xce\xfa\xed\xfe", _UNIVERSAL)

# Runtime and well-known library assemblies are not the developer's code.
_DOTNET_FRAMEWORK = ("system.", "microsoft.", "mscorlib", "netstandard", "windowsbase", "presentationcore",
                     "presentationframework", "mono.", "newtonsoft.", "unityengine.")


def _magic(path: Path) -> bytes | None:
    try:
        with path.open("rb") as f:
            head = f.read(8)
    except OSError:
        return None
    magic = next((m for m in _MAGICS if head.startswith(m)), None)
    # Java class files share the CAFEBABE magic with universal Mach-O; their version field is far
    # larger than any realistic architecture count (seen as DebugProbesKt.bin in a real APK).
    if magic == _UNIVERSAL and (len(head) < 8 or int.from_bytes(head[4:8], "big") > 20):
        return None
    return magic


def _binaries(target: Path) -> list[Path]:
    if target.is_file():
        return [target] if _magic(target) else []
    found = []
    base_depth = len(target.parts)
    for directory, dirs, names in os.walk(target, followlinks=False):
        if len(Path(directory).parts) - base_depth >= MAX_DEPTH:
            dirs.clear()
        for name in sorted(names):
            path = Path(directory) / name
            if not path.is_symlink() and path.is_file() and path.stat().st_size <= MAX_SIZE and _magic(path):
                found.append(path)
                if len(found) >= MAX_BINARIES:
                    return found
    return found


def _rel(path: Path, root: Path) -> str:
    return path.relative_to(root).as_posix() if path != root else path.name


def _elf_sections(binary: _Binary) -> set[str]:
    head = binary.read(0, 64)
    wide, endian = head[4] == 2, "<" if head[5] == 1 else ">"
    if wide:
        shoff = struct.unpack_from(endian + "Q", head, 40)[0]
        entsize, count, strndx = struct.unpack_from(endian + "HHH", head, 58)
    else:
        shoff = struct.unpack_from(endian + "I", head, 32)[0]
        entsize, count, strndx = struct.unpack_from(endian + "HHH", head, 46)
    if not shoff or not count or count > 4096 or strndx >= count:
        return set()
    table = binary.read(shoff, entsize * count)

    def section(index):
        entry = table[index * entsize:(index + 1) * entsize]
        if wide:
            name, _, _, _, offset, size = struct.unpack_from(endian + "IIQQQQ", entry)
        else:
            name, _, _, _, offset, size = struct.unpack_from(endian + "IIIIII", entry)
        return name, offset, size

    _, str_offset, str_size = section(strndx)
    strings = binary.read(str_offset, min(str_size, 1024 * 1024))
    names = set()
    for i in range(count):
        name_index = section(i)[0]
        end = strings.find(b"\0", name_index)
        names.add(strings[name_index:end].decode("ascii", "replace"))
    return names


def _contains(path: Path, markers: tuple[bytes, ...], limit: int | None = None) -> set[bytes]:
    """Which markers appear in the file (or its first `limit` bytes), reading in chunks."""
    found, tail = set(), b""
    read = 0
    with path.open("rb") as f:
        while chunk := f.read(8 * 1024 * 1024):
            data = tail + chunk
            found |= {m for m in markers if m in data}
            tail, read = data[-64:], read + len(chunk)
            if limit and read >= limit:
                break
    return found


# GCC and Clang store each polymorphic class's mangled name for RTTI, for example
# "10FileReader" or "N4Game6PlayerE". Names from the C++ standard library are ignored.
_ITANIUM = re.compile(rb"\x00((?:N(?:\d{1,3}[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)+E)|(?:\d{1,3}[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*))\x00")
_STD_PREFIXES = (b"St", b"NSt", b"N9__gnu_cxx", b"N10__cxxabiv1", b"N12_GLOBAL__", b"N5boost")
_RTTI_MINIMUM = 5


def _itanium_name(raw: bytes) -> bool:
    """True when raw is a well-formed mangled name: every part's length prefix matches."""
    text = raw.decode("ascii", "replace")
    if text.startswith("N"):
        if not text.endswith("E"):
            return False
        text = text[1:-1]
    i, parts = 0, 0
    while i < len(text):
        j = i
        while j < len(text) and text[j].isdigit():
            j += 1
        if j == i:
            return False
        length = int(text[i:j])
        name = text[j:j + length]
        if length < 3 or len(name) != length or not (name[0].isalpha() or name[0] == "_"):
            return False
        parts, i = parts + 1, j + length
    return parts > 0


def _itanium_class_names(path: Path) -> int:
    names, tail = set(), b""
    with path.open("rb") as f:
        while chunk := f.read(8 * 1024 * 1024):
            data = tail + chunk
            names |= {m.group(1) for m in _ITANIUM.finditer(data)
                      if not m.group(1).startswith(_STD_PREFIXES) and _itanium_name(m.group(1))}
            tail = data[-256:]
    return len(names)


def _native_names(path: Path, magic: bytes) -> list[str]:
    """What a native binary gives away: symbol tables, debug info and class-name metadata."""
    exposed = []
    with _Binary(path) as binary:
        if magic == b"MZ":
